## 1. THE SUBJECT, IN FIVE LINES

`DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_isa)` in `src/devices/dev_lca.c` (Alpha `alphabook1`, the 21066 sparse ISA window):
`relative_addr >>= 5` gives the ISA port P (the five discarded bits are k); `if (len == 0) return 0;`;
`ofs = (P & 3) % len`; on a WRITE it forwards `data[ofs]` as ONE byte to port P; on a READ it fetches one byte
and fills `data[0..len)` with that byte at `ofs` and zeros elsewhere.  For every `len >= 4`, `ofs == P & 3`.
The 352-row parent probe drives instruction widths 1/2/4/8 (lanes 0-3, both directions).  The new width row
`regress/lca_widewrite_probe.py` drives ONE PROM GETENV write of 16 bytes (`store_buf`) into the ns16550 scratch
register at port 0x2ff: cell (C=16, k=0, lane 3), and it catches the two variants pass 6 found (`&& len <= 8`
on the write arm; `len == 0 || len > 8` as the guard) -- base 3/3, each variant 2/3.

`store_buf` issues chunks of 1024/256/64/16/4/1 bytes, each only at an address aligned to its size and never
crossing 1 KB, so the wide cells a guest can reach are: C=16 (k in {0,16}, lanes 0-3), C=64 (k=0, lanes 0,2),
C=256 (k=0, lane 0), C=1024 (k=0, lane 0) -- twelve, or EIGHT width/lane cells once the discarded k is collapsed.

## 2. THE RULES IN DISPUTE, inlined

* **Floor clause (d)**: a detector must distinguish the fix from a variant that keeps the guard but nulls its
  effect -- a predicate qualified (`if (X)` -> `if (X && Y)`), a bound relaxed.  Bounded by DEPENDS-ON: the
  predicates dominating the edited lines, and operand swaps from lvalues the guarded body reads.
* **Joint cells (2026-09-04)**: *"DEPENDS-ON enumerates JOINT cells over the guard's operands and the body-read
  lvalues EXACTLY WHEN THE GUARDED BODY'S OWN CODE DISTINGUISHES THEM ... A value or combination the body never
  tests as a case is DISCLOSED under clause (e)."*
* **Clause (e)**: the probe header names what it is designed to catch; a variant outside the stated contract is
  disclosed debt.  **A breach OF the contract (an in-contract variant undetected) is itself L2.**
* **Pass 6's ruling (flagship, 2026-09-17), verbatim:** *"`&byte, len` diverges WITHIN the probed width set
  (catchable, so a contract line suffices); codex's `len<=8` diverges only OUTSIDE it (uncatchable, so it cannot
  be discharged that way)."* ... *"The probe is instruction-driven and the widest Alpha store, STQ, is 8 bytes --
  no instruction-driven row can ever express len > 8."*
* **Class closure**: two consecutive passes finding breaches of ONE class owe the class closure in ONE diff.
* **Stopping rule**: in-round fixes are for MEASURED FALSE PASSES (floor) and WRONG RECORDS; everything else is
  FILED as coverage debt.

## 3. THE MEASUREMENT (the Opus seat, 102 legs on unmodified alphabook1, every value predicted before it ran)

Variants, each ONE edit to the committed function:
`va` write arm `&& len <= 16` | `vb` guard `|| len > 16` | `vc` write arm `&& (len <= 8 || (P & 3) == 3)` |
`vd` `% (len > 8 ? 8 : len)` | `ve` guard `|| (len > 8 && P < 0x200)` | `vf` `data[len > 8 ? 0 : ofs]` |
`vg` before the shift: `if (len > 8 && (relative_addr & 0x10)) return 0;` | `vh` read loop `i < len && i < 8` |
`vt1` "aligned only": `len > 8 && (relative_addr & 0x1f)` refused | `vt2` refuse a wide chunk that continues the
previous access | `vw64`, `vw256`: write arm `&& len <= 64` / `<= 256`.

Cells (the same GETENV instrument, one session each): `frozen` = the shipped row (16, k0, lane 3, SCR 0x2ff);
`k16` SCR at k=16; `l0w16`/`l0w64` MCR 0x2fc lane 0 at C=16/64; `l2w64` MSR 0x2fe lane 2 at C=64; `low16` 8259 IMR
0x21 lane 1; `mc2` one 32-byte GETENV = two 16-byte chunks (0x2fe k16 lane 2, then 0x2ff k0 lane 3);
`kbc16/256/1024` the 8042 command byte via port 0x60 at C=16/256/1024.  Score = rows green / rows; `-` = not run.

    cell     base  va    vb    vc    vd    ve    vf    vg    vh    vt1   vt2   vw64  vw256
    frozen   3/3   3/3   3/3   3/3   3/3   3/3   2/3   3/3   3/3   3/3   3/3   3/3   3/3

**Read it this way:** the shipped row (`frozen`) detects only `vf`; `vd`, `vh` are equivalent everywhere; every
other variant is detected at some OTHER reachable cell by the same instrument.  The pair {mc2, kbc1024} detects
all nine escapes plus `vf`.  The parent's 352 rows score every variant 352/352 by construction (each added
predicate is gated on `len > 8`; the parent never drives a GETENV).

## 4. FINDINGS, with my disposition on each

F1  [all nine] every Q1 prediction held; now MEASURED (section 3).  ACCEPTED.
F2  [codex, all] the twelve-cell count is exact for store_buf; eight width/lane cells once k collapses (codex walked
    all 174,080 start-address/width pairs).  ACCEPTED.
F3  [all nine] L4: the parent header's *"The width cell is therefore COVERED, not merely disclosed"* -- one cell of
    twelve is rowed.  ACCEPTED -> corrected in place.
F4  [all nine] L4: the contract's clause (iii), *"the others share the same `len > 8' predicate"* -- refuted by va,
    vb and the ladder.  ACCEPTED -> corrected in place.
F5  [grok, glm, deepseek, minimax, codex, opus5] the contract's *"DESIGNED TO CATCH: any guard that declines a
    len > 8 WRITE ... in either the guard form or the write-arm-conjunct form"* is falsified by va, vb, vc, ve, vg,
    vt1, vt2 -- each such a guard, each undetected.  ACCEPTED as a contract breach -> the contract is narrowed to
    what the rows measure, and every measured escape is named with the cell that detects it.
F6  [five seats; glm dissents] `wide alive` is survive-only: a reinstated process-ending call at len > 8 leaves t4
    unprinted, so `wide getenv` reds with it.  ACCEPTED (mechanical); glm's dissent REJECTED.
F7  [codex, grok, agy; opus5 and kimi disagree] `wide live` is not a DEVICE signature -- the scratch register is a
    plain stored byte.  REFINED: it is a valid round-trip control (no RAM is mapped in this window), and the label
    should say "round-trip control"; `wide getenv`'s 0x44 is the RAM discriminator.  L4-minor, corrected in place.
F8  [grok; agy was wrong] the earlier Opus GETENV replica (port 0x3ff, a3=64) issued 16-byte chunks, not 64
    (0x7fe0 is not 64-aligned).  ACCEPTED.
F9  [agy, grok, opus5 by census; packet seats could not settle] GETENV/store_buf is the only len > 8 producer into
    this window on alphabook1: no PHYSICAL producer (pccmos only, 1 byte), no bus master, no wide read.  ACCEPTED.
F10 [opus5] CONSTRUCTION GAP: nothing asserts the GETENV length exceeds 8 -- `load_const(A3, 16)` edited to 4 makes
    a len-4 row that base and both pass-6 variants pass silently.  ACCEPTED -> owed with the row's next edit (a
    two-chunk cell such as `mc2` pins the width by construction).
F11 [codex] a single session sweeping several cells is unsound: a variant that falls into the read arm writes
    zeros into store_buf's SOURCE (the emulator's boot string), so later GETENVs see a shorter string.  REFINED:
    sound only with an ORDER in which any clobbering variant is already detected (opus5: {mc2 then kbc1024}).
F12 [codex] the probe exits 0 on FAIL (a runner must parse WIDEWRITE_RESULT); contract (i) conflates byte
    SELECTION with DELIVERY of the other bytes.  ACCEPTED as records.
F13 [kimi] a GETENV to a misaligned a2 is issued as sixteen 1-byte writes, all forwarded -> lcawidefold's "15 of 16
    dropped" is alignment-dependent.  ACCEPTED, recorded on that row.
F14 [grok] the nested forward has no PHYSICAL flag and works only because ptbr==0 identity-maps it -- the open row
    lcaisaphys.  ACCEPTED, recorded on that row.
F15 [glm] option: a direct-call unit harness covering every cell; [kimi] a static lint tripwire; [opus5] fold the
    closure into lcawidefold's eventual fix.  FILED as options, not adopted here.

## 5. DISAGREEMENTS -- please adjudicate your OWN side

**D1 -- are va/vb/vc/ve/vg/vt1/vt2 floor breaches (L2, class closure owed now) or clause-(e) debt?**
* L2 -- **codex**: *"I treat pass 6 as a specific exception establishing coverage obligations for the reachable
  width class, rather than an exception for the literal constant 8. Under that interpretation, moving the cutoff to
  16 cannot discharge the obligation."*  **glm**: pass 6 governs, bounded to dimensions "whose producible values
  are all witnessable"; the letter read literally "would empty clause (d) of its own named example".  **kimi**:
  "distinguishes" means OBSERVABLE behaviour varies -- lane selects the forwarded byte, port the destination -- so
  vc/ve are L2 on either rule.  **deepseek, minimax**: all five are inside the contract's own "any", reachable, and
  undetected.
* DEBT -- **opus5** (measured): *"pass 6's 'uncatchable' is a condition on clause (e): a contract line can only
  discharge a variant that some shipped instrument can reach ... It is now met, measured: every escape is killed
  by the GETENV instrument at another cell."*  **agy**: the constants 16/64/0x200/0x10 introduce partitions the
  body never induces; pass 6 read literally "would create an infinite regress".  **grok**: the letter governs ROW
  demand; one reachable len > 8 WRITE row discharged pass 6's cell; "(c) is the closest call for a real L2 row."

**MY RULING (provisional -- fable5 rules LAST): E-DEBT-PLUS-L4, and I adopt the measuring seat's reading.**
Warrant: pass 6's own reason is about CAPABILITY -- *"no instruction-driven row can ever express len > 8"*.  The
body tests `len` only against 0 and `% len` separates {1},{2},{3},{>=4}, so every width >= 4 is one body class,
already driven at lanes 0-3 by the parent at len 4 and 8.  The shipped GETENV instrument now reaches every wide
cell (section 3), so each escape is dischargeable by a contract line naming the cell that detects it.  That
reading is bounded; codex's is too (by the producer's eight cells), and **I am least sure of this ruling on vc**,
where lane is a partition the body does distinguish.  **Consequences:** correct F3/F4/F5/F7/F12 in place; FILE the
two-cell strengthening {mc2, kbc1024} (measured to detect every escape, and it also closes F10) as coverage debt
for its own round -- or, if you rule L2, it is the class closure's minimal form and I want your cell set.

**D2** `wide alive` alone-red: glm (yes, on the crash class) vs five seats (no).  Ruled: no (F6).
**D3** `wide live` a device signature: opus5/kimi/minimax (yes, in context) vs codex/grok/agy (no).  Ruled: F7.

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is

else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

far more to this panel than a timeout.

66-/*
--
95- *  A first draft of this split used the 4 KB mask and a review seat produced the
96- *  counterexample: with a 1 KB mapping, a four-byte access at 0x13fe crosses a translation
97- *  boundary but NOT a 4 KB one, so the loop never fired and the defect survived exactly
98- *  where MEMORY_NOT_FULL_PAGE says it is most likely.  Splitting finer is always SAFE --
99- *  each chunk is then inside one mapping for every translator here, and the worker's own
100- *  host-page arithmetic is unchanged -- so the only cost is more worker calls on the slow
101- *  path for a multi-page translated access, which is rare (large transfers are PHYSICAL
102- *  and exempt).  Correctness at a known granule beats a faster one that is wrong on ARM.
103- */
104-#define	MRW_SPLIT_MASK		0x3ff
105-
106-/*  The one-page worker; its name is derived from MEMORY_RW because cpu_alpha.c includes
107:    this file TWICE in one translation unit (alpha_userland_memory_rw, then alpha_memory_rw
108-    via tmp_alpha_tail.c), so a plainly named static would be a redefinition.  */
109-#ifndef MRW_CAT2
110-#define	MRW_CAT2(a,b)		a##b
111-#define	MRW_CAT(a,b)		MRW_CAT2(a,b)
112-#endif
113-#define	MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE	MRW_CAT(MEMORY_RW, _one_page)
114-
115-/*
116- *  The one relationship between the two that MUST hold, asserted at COMPILE TIME because it
117- *  cannot be asserted at run time by the offline differential.
118- *
119- *  MEM_ALPHA is never compiled by that differential -- its fixture is 4 KB-specific -- so a
--
148- *  running off the end of a host allocation.
149- *
150- *  It is ONE BOUNDARY IN 1024, not 256: MRW_SPLIT_MASK is 0x3ff, so the split granule is
151- *  1024 bytes and 1 MB / 1024 = 1024.  The old "256" came from dividing 1 MB by a 4 KB page
152- *  -- a granule this split does not use, as the note above says: "The granule the SPLIT
153- *  needs is a different one."  (And per that note the real page here is 8 KB anyway.)
154- *
155- *  Two properties of the memblock split, both MEASURED:
156- *    - post-#430 it fires only on PHYSICAL accesses.  A TRANSLATED 4096-byte write across a
157- *      1 MB boundary is chunked at the 1024-byte SPLIT granule, so it costs 4 translations
158- *      and 4 host lookups and never reaches here; PHYSICAL costs 0 and 2.  A chunk of at
159- *      most one granule, granule-aligned, cannot cross 1 MB.
160: *    - its head can NEVER be a device, in either version: device dispatch `goto
161: *      do_return_ok`s first.  Measured with a device straddling the memblock boundary --
162: *      one device call, offset 0xffe, len 4, zero host lookups.
163- *
164- *  A LOOP, not recursion.  A recursive split costs a real frame per chunk when the compiler
165- *  does NOT eliminate the tail call -- measured at -O0, 1,834,896 bytes of stack for a 16 MB
166- *  access -- and a large enough access then dies on the stack rather than returning an
167- *  answer.
168- *
169- *  CORRECTION, and it is the reason this paragraph is worded carefully: the first draft said
170- *  "gcc does not turn the tail call into a loop at -O2".  MEASURED, IT DOES -- gcc 15.2.1
171- *  performs the sibling-call optimisation at -O2 and -O3, leaving a 96-byte span.  So the
172- *  loop is still the right implementation (it does not depend on an optimisation being
173- *  applied, and -O0 builds and other compilers are real), but the justification was wrong,
174- *  and the row that certified it was VACUOUS until the differential was given
--

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""lca_widewrite_probe.py -- the width row #530 owed (lcalenzerodrop).  MEASURED 2026-09-18.

WHAT IT PINS.  `#530`'s instruction-driven detector, lca_isalane_probe.py, cannot express a store wider
than 8 bytes -- the widest Alpha store, STQ, is 8 bytes (its own header, :148-151).  So a guard that
declines only len > 8 swept all 352 of its rows: `#530`'s KILLLEN8 leg (`len == 0 || len > 8') and the
`&& len <= 8' write-arm conjunct both scored 352/352.  CLOSE6_VERDICT.md pre-committed the disposition:
*escapes AND len > 8 is guest-reachable -> L2, add a len > 8 row in-round.*  This is that row.

THE PATH -- the NON-INSTRUCTION one, because no instruction can reach it:
    CALL_PAL 0x3fffffe      the word machine_alpha.c:189 stores at PROM_ENTRY_PADDR;
                            cpu_alpha_palcode.c:519-520 dispatches it to alpha_prom_call
      a0 = 0x22             PROM_R_GETENV          (thirdparty/alpha_prom.h:69)
      a1 = 0x8              PROM_E_BOOTED_OSFLAGS  (:85) -> s = boot_string_argument, set by -o
      a2 = k0(SP(0x2ff))    the ns16550 SCR, 64-bit K0SEG, loaded via put d + LDQ
      a3 = 16               len = min(a3, strlen(s)+1) = min(16, 17) = 16   (palcode.c:141-143)

       passes 16 (memory.c:839-852) -> lca_isa: port 0x2ff, ofs = (0x2ff & 3) % 16 = 3 -> s[3] = 'D'

in PRETABLE.out at 14:59:49, BEFORE any run, and every value matched):

the CALL_PAL was executed inline WITHOUT loading ra, so the PROM call "returned" to address 0, phase 4

import os, pty, re, select, sys, time

BIN     = sys.argv[1]
LABEL   = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "?"
MACH    = "alphabook1"
BOOTSTR = "ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOP"          # strlen 16, so len = min(a3=16, 17) = 16
DUMMY   = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), "alpha_dummy.bin")   # as the parent

# ---- encoder, copied from lca_isalane_probe.py so the encodings are identical
ZERO, T0, T1, T2, T3, T4 = 31, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5
A0, A1, A2, A3 = 16, 17, 18, 19       # Alpha ABI a0..a3 = r16..r19.  NOT defined in the parent probe,
                                      # which deliberately names only T0..T6 -- defined here, on purpose.
def mem(op, ra, rb, disp):
    assert -32768 <= disp <= 32767, "displacement %d out of range" % disp
    return (op << 26) | (ra << 21) | (rb << 16) | (disp & 0xffff)
def LDA (ra, disp, rb=ZERO): return mem(0x08, ra, rb, disp)
def LDAH(ra, disp, rb=ZERO): return mem(0x09, ra, rb, disp)
def LDBU(ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x0a, ra, rb, disp)
def STB (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x0e, ra, rb, disp)
def LDQ (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x29, ra, rb, disp)
CALL_PAL_PROM = (0x00 << 26) | 0x3fffffe      # == 0x03fffffe, the word machine_alpha.c:189 stores

def load_const(reg, val):                     # 32-bit only, exactly as the parent
    lo = val & 0xffff
    hi = (val >> 16) & 0xffff
    if lo >= 0x8000:
        hi = (hi + 1) & 0xffff
    ops = [LDAH(reg, hi if hi < 0x8000 else hi - 0x10000)]
    if lo:
        ops.append(LDA(reg, lo if lo < 0x8000 else lo - 0x10000, reg))
    return ops

SENT = 0x0bad1d8a                             # the parent's pre-seed: a faulting load leaves it
def sent(reg): return load_const(reg, SENT)

K0SEG       = 0xfffffc0000000000
def k0(p):  return K0SEG | p
LCA_PCI_SIO = 0x1c0000000
def SP(port): return LCA_PCI_SIO + (port << 5)
SCRATCH, CODE = 0x01020000, 0x01040000

# ---- harness: the parent's session(), with ONE change -- `-o BOOTSTR' -- in a separate function so
#      the parent's 352 rows cannot be affected by it
def session(cmds, settle, spin):
    pid, fd = pty.fork()
    if pid == 0:
        os.execv(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "alpha", "-e", MACH, "-M", "64",
                       "-o", BOOTSTR, "0x20000000:" + DUMMY])
        os._exit(127)
    buf = ""
    def rd(t):
        nonlocal buf
        end = time.time() + t
        while time.time() < end:
            r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], 0.1)
            if not r:
                continue
            try:
                d = os.read(fd, 65536)
                if not d:
                    return False
                buf += d.decode("latin-1", "replace")
            except OSError:
                return False
        return True
    alive = rd(settle)
    for i, c in enumerate(cmds):
        try:
            os.write(fd, (c + "\n").encode("latin-1"))
        except OSError:
            alive = False; break
        if not rd(spin if i == len(cmds) - 1 else 0.45):
            alive = False; break
    try:
        os.write(fd, b"quit\n"); rd(0.5)
    except OSError:
        pass
    try:
        os.close(fd)
    except OSError:
        pass
    return buf.replace("\r", ""), alive

def build(consts, phases):                    # copied verbatim in behaviour
    prog = load_const(T0, SCRATCH)
    lens = [len(prog)]
    for ops, _ in phases:
        prog += ops
        lens.append(len(ops))
    cmds  = ["put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (SCRATCH + 8 * i, v) for i, v in enumerate(consts)]
    cmds += ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (k0(CODE) + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(prog)]
    cmds += ["pc=0x%x" % k0(CODE), "step %d" % lens[0]]
    for (ops, pr), n in zip(phases, lens[1:]):
        cmds.append("step %d" % n)
        if pr is not None:
            cmds.append("print %s" % pr)
    return cmds

def printed(t, reg):
    m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
    return None if not m else int(m.group(1), 16)

rows = []
def row(name, got, want, note=""):
    ok = (got == want)
    rows.append((name, ok))
    print("%-14s %-24s %-24s %-56s %s" % (name, str(got), str(want), note, "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
    return ok

# ---- the session
SCR, PRESET = 0x2ff, 0x5a
WANT = ord(BOOTSTR[(SCR & 3) % 16])          # s[3] == 'D' == 0x44 -- derived, not typed in
#  *** RUN 1 WAS AN L1 CONSTRUCTION FAILURE AND THIS IS ITS FIX. ***  alpha_prom_call ENDS WITH
#  `cpu->pc = cpu->cd.alpha.r[ALPHA_RA]' (cpu_alpha_palcode.c:153) -- the real PROM entry is reached
#  by `jsr', which loads ra.  Run 1 executed the CALL_PAL inline WITHOUT setting ra, so the call

#  (logs/run1_L1_no_return_address/).  ra is now loaded with the address of the instruction AFTER the
#  CALL_PAL -- COMPUTED FROM THE LAYOUT, not typed in, so an edit to any phase cannot silently strand it.
RA = 26                                       # Alpha ra = r26
p1 = [LDQ(T2, 0, T0)] + load_const(T1, PRESET) + [STB(T1, 0, T2)]           # preset the SCR
p2 = [LDQ(T2, 0, T0)] + sent(T3) + [LDBU(T3, 0, T2)]                         # CONTROL: reads back
p3 = (load_const(A0, 0x22) + load_const(A1, 0x8) + [LDQ(A2, 0, T0)]
      + load_const(A3, 16) + [LDQ(RA, 8, T0), CALL_PAL_PROM])               # GETENV, 16-byte store
p4 = [LDQ(T2, 0, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T2)]                         # the SCR afterwards
RET = k0(CODE) + 4 * (len(load_const(T0, SCRATCH)) + len(p1) + len(p2) + len(p3))
consts = [k0(SP(SCR)), RET]                   # slot 0: the SCR; slot 8: where the PROM call returns
phases = [(p1, None), (p2, "t3"), (p3, None), (p4, "t4")]

print("lca_widewrite -- binary=%s label=%s machine=%s -o %s" % (BIN, LABEL, MACH, BOOTSTR))
cmds = build(consts, phases)
txt, alive = session(cmds, 3.0, 4.0)
#  MIRRORS THE PARENT'S run(): a session whose prints did not all arrive TIMED OUT rather than
#  measured, and is retried ONCE with tripled patience -- "the machine may be loaded".  Without this,
#  a slow run under contention reads as a FAILED ROW: a phantom red that the row did not earn.
retried = False
if printed(txt, "t3") is None or printed(txt, "t4") is None:
    retried = True
    txt, alive = session(cmds, 3.0 * 3, 4.0 * 3)
print("WIDEWRITE_RETRIED=%s" % ("yes" if retried else "none"))
if os.environ.get("WIDEWRITE_RAW"):
    open(os.environ["WIDEWRITE_RAW"], "w", encoding="utf-8").write(txt)

t3, t4 = printed(txt, "t3"), printed(txt, "t4")
row("wide alive", "alive" if alive else "died", "alive",
    "the guest survives an inline CALL_PAL 0x3fffffe")
row("wide live", t3, PRESET,
    "LIVENESS + DEVICE SIGNATURE: a 1-byte store round-trips through the SCR")
row("wide getenv", t4, WANT,
    "a 16-byte store_buf write lands s[3] on the SCR; M2 (`&& len <= 8') leaves the PRESET")
ok = sum(1 for _, o in rows if o)
print("WIDEWRITE_RESULT=%d/%d" % (ok, len(rows)))
print("WIDEWRITE_DONE")

/*
 *  Copyright (C) 2006-2011  Anders Gavare.  All rights reserved.
 *
 *  Redistribution and use in source and binary forms, with or without

 *
 *  1. Redistributions of source code must retain the above copyright
 *     notice, this list of conditions and the following disclaimer.
 *  2. Redistributions in binary form must reproduce the above copyright  
 *     notice, this list of conditions and the following disclaimer in the 
 *     documentation and/or other materials provided with the distribution.
 *  3. The name of the author may not be used to endorse or promote products
 *     derived from this software without specific prior written permission.
 *
 *  THIS SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED BY THE AUTHOR AND CONTRIBUTORS ``AS IS'' AND
 *  ANY EXPRESS OR IMPLIED WARRANTIES, INCLUDING, BUT NOT LIMITED TO, THE
 *  IMPLIED WARRANTIES OF MERCHANTABILITY AND FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE
 *  ARE DISCLAIMED.  IN NO EVENT SHALL THE AUTHOR OR CONTRIBUTORS BE LIABLE   
 *  FOR ANY DIRECT, INDIRECT, INCIDENTAL, SPECIAL, EXEMPLARY, OR CONSEQUENTIAL
 *  DAMAGES (INCLUDING, BUT NOT LIMITED TO, PROCUREMENT OF SUBSTITUTE GOODS
 *  OR SERVICES; LOSS OF USE, DATA, OR PROFITS; OR BUSINESS INTERRUPTION)
 *  HOWEVER CAUSED AND ON ANY THEORY OF LIABILITY, WHETHER IN CONTRACT, STRICT
 *  LIABILITY, OR TORT (INCLUDING NEGLIGENCE OR OTHERWISE) ARISING IN ANY WAY
 *  OUT OF THE USE OF THIS SOFTWARE, EVEN IF ADVISED OF THE POSSIBILITY OF
 *  SUCH DAMAGE.

 *
 *  COMMENT: LCA PCI bus, for Alpha machines

#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>

#include "bus_isa.h"
#include "bus_pci.h"
#include "cpu.h"
#include "device.h"
#include "emul.h"
#include "interrupt.h"
#include "machine.h"
#include "memory.h"
#include "misc.h"

#include "thirdparty/alpha_lcareg.h"

#define	LCA_ISA_BASE	(LCA_PCI_SIO + 0x10000000)
#define	LCA_ISA_MEMBASE	(LCA_PCI_SIO + 0x18000000)


/*  #516: warn-once latch bits, one per stub.  PER-INSTANCE, reached through
    `interrupt->extra' -- see the comment on lca_interrupt_assert().  */
#define	LCA_W_IRQ_ASSERT	0	/*  #516  */
#define	LCA_W_IRQ_DEASSERT	1	/*  #516  */
#define	LCA_W_IOC_CONF_CFG1	2	/*  #518: IOC_CONF<1> set -- CFG AD<1:0> = 10/11, not a cycle type  */
#define	LCA_W_IOC_WMASK0	3	/*  #518: unsupported W_MASK0 encoding declined  */
#define	LCA_W_IOC_WMASK1	4	/*  #518: unsupported W_MASK1 encoding declined  */
#define	LCA_W_IOC_WEN		5	/*  #518: a DMA window enabled -- translation not modelled  */
#define	LCA_W_IOC_DEF_W	6	/*  #518: default-arm write declined  */
#define	LCA_W_IOC_DEF_R	7	/*  #518: default-arm read answered with zero  */

struct lca_data {
	struct pci_data		*pci_data;

	unsigned int		warned;		/*  #516  */

	uint64_t		ioc_conf;
	uint64_t		tlb_enable;
	uint64_t		window_base_0;
	uint64_t		window_mask_0;
	uint64_t		window_t_base_0;
	uint64_t		window_base_1;
	uint64_t		window_mask_1;
	uint64_t		window_t_base_1;
};


/*
 *  lca_interrupt_assert():
 *
 *  Line 0 = ISA interrupt.
 */
void lca_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
{
	struct lca_data *d = (struct lca_data *) interrupt->extra;	/*  #516  */

	/*  #516: this was `fatal(); exit(1)' with NO GUARD -- not a refused value,
	    an unimplemented path -- and DEVINIT below installs it as the ISA
	    interrupt callback that `bus_isa_init' wires to BUS_ISA_IDE0|IDE1.  So an
	    ordinary ISA interrupt ended the host, needing no unusual value anywhere.
	    MEASURED on two independent guest paths: one store to the 8042 command
	    port (regress/lca_irqstub_probe.py, 3/4) and three byte stores to the
	    ns16550 on IRQ 4 (regress/lca_irqstub_uart_probe.py, 2/4).

	    *** THE STUB IS NOT REPLACED BY A FORWARD, BECAUSE A FORWARD WOULD BE A
	    NO-OP.  ALPHA HAS NO INTERRUPT DELIVERY AT ALL. ***  `cd.alpha.irq_asserted'
	    is written by `cpu_alpha.c' and read NOWHERE.  `cpu_dyntrans.c' turns a
	    pending interrupt into a delivered one for FIVE architectures -- ARM and
	    M88K and PPC through their own `irq_asserted', MIPS through the COP0
	    exception model, and SH through `int_to_assert' -- and ALPHA IS ABSENT
	    FROM THAT LIST.  (An earlier draft of this comment said "ARM, M88K and
	    PPC only": that enumeration was made by grepping for `irq_asserted', which
	    is a CORRELATE of delivering rather than the property, and it missed the
	    two architectures that deliver by another mechanism.  The Alpha conclusion
	    is unchanged and is strengthened -- it is the outlier among five, not
	    three.)  Calling `cpu_interrupt()' here would set
	    a field nothing consumes.  Implementing delivery is a SUBSYSTEM -- the
	    dyntrans dispatch, the PALcode entry and the IPL check -- and is its own
	    round, not this one.

	    So the disposition is DECLINE plus a bounded announce, and what it buys is
	    stated narrowly on purpose: THE HOST STOPS DYING.  Interrupts do not start
	    working; they were never delivered on this architecture and still are not.

	    The latch is PER-INSTANCE (`interrupt->extra'), not function-static: a
	    function-static bit would let one guest access to one device instance
	    silence the diagnostic for every other instance, which is a class already
	    filed against this tree.  */
	if (d != NULL && !(d->warned & (1u << LCA_W_IRQ_ASSERT))) {	/*  #516  */
		d->warned |= 1u << LCA_W_IRQ_ASSERT;
		fatal("[ lca: ISA interrupt assert not implemented (Alpha has no "
		    "interrupt delivery); ignored  (once) ]\n");
	}
}


/*
 *  lca_interrupt_deassert():
 *
 *  Line 0 = ISA interrupt.
 */
void lca_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
{
	struct lca_data *d = (struct lca_data *) interrupt->extra;	/*  #516  */

	/*  #516: the sibling of lca_interrupt_assert() above, same reasoning.  A
	    SEPARATE latch bit, so the two are distinguishable: a driver normally
	    reaches both within one interrupt cycle, and one shared bit would report
	    whichever arrived first and hide the other entirely.  */
	if (d != NULL && !(d->warned & (1u << LCA_W_IRQ_DEASSERT))) {	/*  #516  */
		d->warned |= 1u << LCA_W_IRQ_DEASSERT;
		fatal("[ lca: ISA interrupt deassert not implemented (Alpha has no "
		    "interrupt delivery); ignored  (once) ]\n");
	}
}


DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_pci_conf)
{
	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
	int tag, type1, bus, dev, func, reg;	/*  #515  */
	struct lca_data *d = (struct lca_data *) extra;

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);

	/*
	 *  1. Decompose the address into a tag.
	 *
	 *  According to NetBSD's lca_pci.c, the address is composed like this:
	 *
	 *	addr = tag << 5 | (regoffset & ~0x03) << 5 | 0x3 << 3
	 */
	reg = (relative_addr >> 5) & 0xfc;
	tag = (relative_addr >> 5) & ~0xff;

	/*
	 *  2. Decompose the tag into bus, dev, and func.
	 *
	 *  The tag can be constructed in one of two ways. On the primary
	 *  bus (nr 0):
	 *
	 *	tag = (1 << (device + 11)) | (function << 8);
	 *
	 *  and on other busses, the tag is a normal:
	 *
	 *	tag = (bus << 16) | (device << 11) | (function << 8)
	 */
	/*  printf("tag = 0x%x\n", (int)tag);  */

	/*  #515: `bus' used to be BOTH the configuration CYCLE TYPE and the
	    DESTINATION BUS, and conflating them is the whole defect.  IOC_CONF bit 0
	    selects the cycle type only -- 21066/21066A HRM S6.2.9: "PCI address bits
	    ad<1:0> come directly from configuration cycle type register (IOC_CONF,
	    Section 6.4.2) bits <1:0>" -- and it does NOT carry a bus number.  The
	    destination bus of a type-1 cycle lives in the ADDRESS, per the PCI Local
	    Bus specification's type-1 layout (bus 23..16, dev 15..11, fn 10..8,
	    reg 7..2), which S6.2.9 defers to explicitly.

	    Before this, a guest that merely SET bit 0 -- a value the CONF guard
	    ACCEPTS -- made any subsequent access in this window reach
	    `fatal("TODO. Non-zero bus."); exit(1)'.  MEASURED: two ordinary guest
	    accesses, nothing refused anywhere on the path
	    (regress/lca_confbus_probe.py, 3/4 pre-fix; artifacts/lcaconfbus1kill/).  */
	type1 = d->ioc_conf & 1;	/*  #515  */
	bus = 0;			/*  #515  */

	if (!type1) {			/*  #515  */
		/*  #511: was `0x800 << dev'.  `0x800' is a plain int (2^11) and `dev'
		    reaches 20, so the last iteration evaluates 2^31 -- a SIGNED
		    OVERFLOW, undefined by C99 6.5.7p4, not merely
		    implementation-defined.  MEASURED under UBSAN before the change:
		    "dev_lca.c:125:21: runtime error: left shift of 2048 by 20 places
		    cannot be represented in type 'int'", reached by an ordinary guest
		    ldq into the PCI config window (regress/lca_confbus_probe.py against
		    build-asan; artifacts/lcashiftub/ubsan_HEAD_20260907.raw).

		    `0x800U' makes the operand unsigned, so 2048U << 20 is 0x80000000U --
		    inside unsigned int -- and NOTHING ELSE MOVES.

		    *** THE REASON, CORRECTED: AN EARLIER DRAFT SAID `tag' IS "ALREADY A
		    WIDER UNSIGNED QUANTITY".  THAT IS FALSE -- `tag' is a plain signed
		    `int' (:92) -- AND FOUR PASS-2 SEATS CAUGHT IT.  Worse, the claim was
		    backwards: being WIDER is what would CREATE a difference, not remove
		    one.  Had `tag' been `uint64_t', the old `int' 0x80000000 is NEGATIVE
		    and would SIGN-EXTEND to 0xffffffff80000000 for the `&', while
		    `0x800U << 20' zero-extends to 0x0000000080000000 -- two different
		    masks.  The seats were right about the mechanism and it does not fire
		    here only because of the type.

		    WHAT ACTUALLY HOLDS: both operands are 32-bit, so the `&' is performed
		    at 32 bits either way -- `int & int' before, and after, `tag' converted
		    to `unsigned int' by the usual arithmetic conversions.  The RESULT'S
		    BIT PATTERN IS IDENTICAL; only its type changes, and it is consumed
		    solely as a truth test in `if (...)'.  So no guest-visible value moves,
		    and the repair is owed purely by the LANGUAGE. ***

		    *** THE LOOP THIS SITS IN IS THIS FILE'S OWN GRACEFUL-DEGRADATION
		    PRECEDENT -- the `dev >= 21' arm below has a commented-out
		    fatal()/exit(1) and substitutes `dev = 0' instead, and `lcabadval'
		    cites it as a transferable warrant.  The safe path contained the UB. ***  */
		for (dev=0; dev<21; dev++)
			if (tag & (0x800U << dev))	/*  #511  */
				break;
		if (dev >= 21) {
			/*  fatal("[ LCA: No bus 0 device? TODO ]\n");
			exit(1);  */
			dev = 0;
		}
	} else {
		/*  #515: a type-1 cycle carries its destination in the address.  */
		bus = (tag >> 16) & 0xff;	/*  #515  */
		dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f;	/*  #515  */
	}

	func = (tag >> 8) & 7;
	/*  printf("bus=%i dev=%i func=%i reg=%i\n", bus,dev,func,reg);  */

	/*  #515: a type-1 cycle whose bus field is zero selects NOTHING.  Type-1 is
	    how a bridge is asked to reach a bus BEHIND it; the primary bus is reached
	    with type-0 cycles, whose IDSEL encoding is decoded above.  Forwarding this
	    case would set cur_bus = 0 and hand the access to whichever primary-bus
	    device matched -- `i82378zb' is registered at 0:7:0 by DEVINIT below -- so
	    simply decoding `bus' and stopping there would replace a host kill with a
	    NEW MISROUTING.  Refuse it instead, matching `bus_pci_data_access''s own
	    empty-slot contract (bus_pci.c, the `dev == NULL' arm: all-ones at register
	    0, zero elsewhere) so the guest sees the ordinary "nothing there" answer.
	    Writes are discarded, which leaves backend state untouched.  */
	if (type1 && bus == 0) {		/*  #515  */
		odata = reg == 0? (uint64_t) -1 : 0;
	} else {
		/*  Pass PCI accesses onto bus_pci:  */
		bus_pci_setaddr(cpu, d->pci_data, bus, dev, func, reg);
		bus_pci_data_access(cpu, d->pci_data, writeflag == MEM_READ?
		    &odata : &idata, len, writeflag);
	}

	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);

	return 1;
}


DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_isa)
{
	unsigned int ofs, i;
	uint8_t byte;

	relative_addr >>= 5;

	/*
	 *  #530: the byte for ISA port P sits at buffer position (P & 3) % len
	 *  on BOTH arms -- the lane for the 21066's own len 4/8 accesses, and
	 *  the rule the write arm already applied wherever the old guard let
	 *  it run.  That guard's `ofs > len' exit(1) ended the host on a guest
	 *  byte access to a port 2 or 3 mod 4 or a word access to a port 3 mod
	 *  4 (ungated BWX; and at a port 3 mod 4 also the 1- or 2-byte head
	 *  of an unaligned load or store that memory_rw splits at 1 KB),
	 *  and the read arm discarded the
	 *  fetched byte at ofs == len.
	 *  One byte is forwarded whatever len is (the size field a<4:3> is
	 *  not decoded here).  len 0 has no producer; refuse it before the %.
	 */
	if (len == 0)
		return 0;
	ofs = (relative_addr & 3) % len;

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
		byte = data[ofs];
		return cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, LCA_ISA_BASE +
		    relative_addr, &byte, 1, writeflag, CACHE_NONE);
	}

	cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, LCA_ISA_BASE + relative_addr,
	    &byte, 1, MEM_READ, CACHE_NONE);






DEVINIT(lca)
{
	char *interrupt_path;
	struct interrupt interrupt_template;
	struct lca_data *d;

	CHECK_ALLOCATION(d = (struct lca_data *) malloc(sizeof(struct lca_data)));
	memset(d, 0, sizeof(struct lca_data));

	/*  Register a PCI bus:  */
	d->pci_data = bus_pci_init(
	    devinit->machine,
	    "TODO: irq"		/*  pciirq: TODO  */,
	    LCA_PCI_SIO,	/*  pci device io offset  */
	    0x00000000,		/*  pci device mem offset: TODO  */
	    0x00000000,		/*  PCI portbase: TODO  */
	    0x00000000,		/*  PCI membase: TODO  */
	    "TODO: pci irq base",	/*  PCI irqbase: TODO  */
	    LCA_ISA_BASE,	/*  ISA portbase  */
	    LCA_ISA_MEMBASE,	/*  ISA membase  */
	    "TODO: irqbase isa");                /*  ISA irqbase: TODO  */

	/*  Add the "sio0" controller (as seen by NetBSD):  */
	bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pci_data, devinit->machine->memory,
	    0, 7, 0, "i82378zb");

	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "lca_pci_conf",
	    LCA_PCI_CONF, 0x20000000, dev_lca_pci_conf_access, (void *)d,
	    DM_DEFAULT, NULL);

	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "lca_isa",
	    LCA_PCI_SIO, 0x10000 << 5, dev_lca_isa_access, (void *)d,
	    DM_DEFAULT, NULL);

	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "lca_ioc",
	    LCA_IOC_BASE, 0x20000000, dev_lca_ioc_access, (void *)d,
	    DM_DEFAULT, NULL);

	CHECK_ALLOCATION(interrupt_path = (char *)
	    malloc(strlen(devinit->machine->path) + 10));
	snprintf(interrupt_path, strlen(devinit->machine->path) + 10,
	    "%s.lca", devinit->machine->path);

	memset(&interrupt_template, 0, sizeof(interrupt_template));
	interrupt_template.line = 0;
	interrupt_template.name = interrupt_path;
	interrupt_template.extra = d;
	interrupt_template.interrupt_assert = lca_interrupt_assert;
	interrupt_template.interrupt_deassert = lca_interrupt_deassert;
	interrupt_handler_register(&interrupt_template);

	bus_isa_init(devinit->machine, interrupt_path,
	    BUS_ISA_IDE0 | BUS_ISA_IDE1, LCA_ISA_BASE, LCA_ISA_MEMBASE);

	return 1;
}

#
#  *** WHAT THE WITNESS (sessions 1-3) IS AND IS NOT.  IT IS A WITNESS, NOT A DETECTOR. ***

#  It establishes, on unmodified `alphabook1' with no disk image and no OS: that the sparse window
#  IS live and does reach ISA devices (`live', 0x55 from the 8042 through the same handler); that a
#  BYTE read of a device register whose port is 1 mod 4 completes successfully and hands the guest
#  zero while the device's own byte is demonstrably present (`ofs1 devsig' + `ofs1 wide'); and that
#  a byte read of a register whose port is 2 or 3 mod 4 ends the host with the arm's own diagnostic.
#
#  It is NOT a detector of any repair.  `alive' greens equally for a decline, for a latched
#  announce, for a fault and for a full implementation, and `ofs1 zero' greens for any fix that
#  hands the byte back.  A fix round owes rows that discriminate those -- at minimum, per the codex
#  design reading (regress/artifacts/lcaisalane/codex_design_20260910.err): a REFUSED access must
#  leave the destination register holding its previous value (the `answer' channel this probe
#  already reads), the refusal must repeat without re-announcing, and a supported lane must still
#  deliver the correctly packed byte at every width.
#
#  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
#  SHAPE, and every element of it is answering a specific objection:
#
#    1.  ONE PROCESS PER OUTCOME, CONTROL FIRST.  A death ends the process, so the two killing
#        lanes get their own runs -- but inside each run the DEVICE SIGNATURE and the WIDE control
#        are taken BEFORE the killing access, so between the access that lives and the access that
#        dies only the WIDTH differs: same address, same window, same process, same instant.
#    2.  ZERO READBACK ALONE PROVES NOTHING, and the design seat said so in those words.  Unmapped
#        space, a missed device and a zero-filled failure buffer all read 0 (`memory_rw.c''s #95
#        pre-zero).  So `ofs1 zero' is never asserted on its own: `ofs1 devsig' reads the SAME
#        register through the flat ISA mapping and gets a nonzero value the device alone produces,
#        and `ofs1 wide' reads it through THIS handler at len 4 and finds the byte sitting in lane
#        1 -- so the byte reaches the packing loop and the loop is what drops it.
#    3.  THE DESTINATION REGISTER IS PRE-SEEDED WITH A SENTINEL before every load.  A load that
#        FAULTS leaves the register untouched (cpu_alpha_instr_loadstore.c returns before
#        writeback when memory_rw fails), so `ofs1 answer' distinguishes "the model answered zero"
#        from "the access failed and the guest kept its old value".  On HEAD it is the former: a
#        FALSE SUCCESS.  This is the discriminator the design seat asked for, taken pre-fix.
#    4.  ATTRIBUTION IS BY THE ARM'S OWN TEXT, NEVER BY EXIT STATUS.  `in lca_isa access function'
#        appears exactly once in src/.  A death for any other reason -- a bad encoding, a different
#        arm -- reds the attrib row instead of passing as a reproduction.
#    5.  EVERY ACCESS IS A GUEST INSTRUCTION.  `lca_isa' is registered DM_DEFAULT, so debugger
#        `dump' would skip the handler entirely and read the pre-zero.  Every `put d' below targets
#        SCRATCH, which is RAM.
#    6.  STAY ON `step'.  Nothing here needs a host timer: the 8042 reply and the UART register
#        file are synchronous with the guest access.
#
#  THE NEGATIVE CONTROL IS ISA PORT 0x300 (`ctl'), which is 0 mod 4 and has NO device behind it.
#  It survives and answers zero.  That is what attributes the two deaths to the LANE rather than to
#  the sparse window, to the byte width, to K0SEG, or to hitting empty ISA space -- and note the
#  two killing ports DO have a device behind them, so the pair brackets the question from both
#  sides.
#
#  Usage:  lca_isalane_probe.py <gxemul-binary> [label]
#          LCAISALANE_RAW=<dir>   -- optional: dump each session's raw pty transcript there
import os, sys, pty, select, time, re

BIN   = sys.argv[1]
LABEL = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "?"
MACH  = "alphabook1"          #  device_add(machine, "lca") is ST_ALPHABOOK1 ONLY
RAW   = os.environ.get("LCAISALANE_RAW", "")

rows = []
def row(name, got, want, note=""):
    ok = (got == want)
    rows.append((name, ok))
    print("%-14s %-24s %-24s %-56s %s" % (name, str(got), str(want), note, "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
    return ok

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- encoder (memory format only)
ZERO, T0, T1, T2, T3, T4, T5, T6 = 31, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7
#  T6 is the highest used.  A probe reaching for an undefined register name dies with a NameError
#  rather than emitting a bad encoding, which is the safe direction (#516 found this the hard way).

def mem(op, ra, rb, disp):
    assert -32768 <= disp <= 32767, "displacement %d out of range" % disp
    return (op << 26) | (ra << 21) | (rb << 16) | (disp & 0xffff)

def LDA (ra, disp, rb=ZERO): return mem(0x08, ra, rb, disp)
def LDAH(ra, disp, rb=ZERO): return mem(0x09, ra, rb, disp)
def LDBU(ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x0a, ra, rb, disp)   # BWX byte load  -> memory_rw len 1
def LDL (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x28, ra, rb, disp)   # longword load  -> memory_rw len 4
def LDQ (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x29, ra, rb, disp)   # quadword load  -> memory_rw len 8
def STB (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x0e, ra, rb, disp)   # BWX byte store -> memory_rw len 1
def LDWU(ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x0c, ra, rb, disp)   # BWX word load  -> memory_rw len 2  (#530)
def STW (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x0d, ra, rb, disp)   # BWX word store -> memory_rw len 2  (#530)
def STL (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x2c, ra, rb, disp)   # longword store -> memory_rw len 4  (#530 pass 2)
def STQ (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x2d, ra, rb, disp)   # quadword store -> memory_rw len 8  (#530 pass 2)
#  LDBU/STB are opcodes 0x0a/0x0e and are decoded with `loadstore_type = 0' in
#  cpu_alpha_instr.c, which selects the 1-byte template in cpu_alpha_instr_loadstore.c -- so the
#  handler under test really does see len == 1.  Not a new capability: lca_irqstub_probe.py already
#  drives STB on this machine, and there is no BWX gate in this tree.

def load_const(reg, val):
    #  32-bit only, and deliberately so: this builds SCRATCH pointers and sentinels, never a
    #  64-bit operand.  Every 64-bit address this probe drives arrives via `put d' + LDQ.
    lo = val & 0xffff
    hi = (val >> 16) & 0xffff
    if lo >= 0x8000:
        hi = (hi + 1) & 0xffff
    ops = [LDAH(reg, hi if hi < 0x8000 else hi - 0x10000)]
    if lo:
        ops.append(LDA(reg, lo if lo < 0x8000 else lo - 0x10000, reg))
    return ops

SENT = 0x0bad1d8a         #  the pre-seed.  A load that FAULTS leaves this in the register.
#  #530: was 0x0bad1dea, whose low byte 0xea has bits 5-6 SET -- so `ofs1 zero', masked to
#  LSR_FIXED (0x60), read a declined load (sentinel kept) as the LSR byte and went GREEN under a
#  decline (measured on the F2 A variant, regress/artifacts/lcaisalane/opus_f2_20260913/).  0x8a
#  has bits 5-6 CLEAR.  Re-pinned FIRST, HEAD re-run 13/16 with the same three reds, THEN the fix
#  (the oracle-correction order; FABLE_F2_RULING_20260914.md R3).
def sent(reg): return load_const(reg, SENT)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- addresses
K0SEG        = 0xfffffc0000000000
def k0(p):   return K0SEG | p

LCA_PCI_SIO  = 0x1c0000000               # alpha_lcareg.h; DEVINIT maps 0x10000<<5 bytes here as
                                         # "lca_isa" -- THE HANDLER UNDER TEST
LCA_ISA_BASE = LCA_PCI_SIO + 0x10000000  # dev_lca.c:47.  bus_isa_init put the real devices here,
                                         # and this is where the handler FORWARDS to.
def SP(port):   return LCA_PCI_SIO  + (port << 5)   # the guest-visible sparse address
def FLAT(port): return LCA_ISA_BASE + port          # the internal flat mapping

#  ---- the ports, and why each one ----------------------------------------------------------
KBC_CMD, KBC_DAT = 0x64, 0x60      # 8042: both 0 mod 4.  0xAA is the standard self-test command
KBC_SELFTEST, KBC_REPLY = 0xAA, 0x55
UART_LSR   = 0x3fd                 # 1 mod 4 -> ofs 1.  DEVICE_ACCESS(ns16550) sets
LSR_FIXED  = 0x60                  #   `d->reg[com_lsr] |= LSR_TXRDY | LSR_TSRE' at the TOP of
                                   #   every access, so 0x20|0x40 is there by construction, with
                                   #   no setup.  Masked to those two bits because LSR_RXRDY (0x01)
                                   #   tracks console input and is the one bit a stray keystroke
                                   #   could flip -- a witness must not have a race in its oracle.
UART_MSR   = 0x3fe                 # 2 mod 4 -> ofs 2.  Same handler unconditionally sets
MSR_FIXED  = 0xb0                  #   MSR_DCD|MSR_DSR|MSR_CTS = 0x80|0x20|0x10, and nothing else
                                   #   ever sets an MSR bit, so this is exact.
UART_SCR   = 0x3ff                 # 3 mod 4 -> ofs 3.  reg 7 falls to the ns16550 `default:' arm,
SCR_VALUE  = 0x5a                  #   which stores and returns the byte -- a plain scratchpad.
NODEV_PORT = 0x300                 # 0 mod 4, and bus_isa_init registers nothing at 0x300.

SCRATCH      = 0x01020000
CODE         = 0x01040000
assert max(SCRATCH, CODE) < 64 * 1024 * 1024, "scratch/code must be inside RAM"

DUMMY = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), "alpha_dummy.bin")
if not os.path.exists(DUMMY):
    with open(DUMMY, "wb") as f:
        f.write(b"\0" * 8)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- session
def session(cmds, settle, spin):
    pid, fd = pty.fork()
    if pid == 0:
        os.execv(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "alpha", "-e", MACH, "-M", "64",
                       "0x20000000:" + DUMMY])
        os._exit(127)
    buf = ""
    def rd(t):
        nonlocal buf
        end = time.time() + t
        while time.time() < end:
            r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], 0.1)
            if not r:
                continue
            try:
                d = os.read(fd, 65536)
                if not d:
                    return False
        os.write(fd, b"quit\n"); rd(0.5)    except OSError:
        pass
    try:
        os.close(fd)
    except OSError:
        pass
    return buf.replace("\r", ""), alive

def printed(t, reg):
    m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
    return None if not m else int(m.group(1), 16)

#  #530 repair 6: `printed' is re.search -- it returns the FIRST print of a register and nothing else.  That is
#  correct for every session written before repair 6, because each prints a given register once.  The 8253 sessions
#  print T3/T4/T5 once per PHASE, ~20 phases per session, so reading them with `printed' would silently hand every
#  row PHASE 1's value -- a whole session vacuous by construction, and GREEN.  `nth' is the positional form (the
#  idiom crime_width_probe.py already uses).  `printed' is deliberately NOT reimplemented in terms of it, so the
#  existing 185 rows cannot move.
def nth(t, reg, n):
    m = re.findall(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
    return None if n >= len(m) else int(m[n], 16)


def n_prints(t, reg):
    return len(re.findall(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t))


def build(consts, phases):
    """consts -> SCRATCH slots 0,8,16,...   phases = list of (ops, printreg|None).
    Returns the debugger command list."""
    prog = load_const(T0, SCRATCH)
    lens = [len(prog)]
    for ops, _ in phases:
        prog += ops
        lens.append(len(ops))
    cmds  = ["put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (SCRATCH + 8 * i, v) for i, v in enumerate(consts)]
    cmds += ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (k0(CODE) + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(prog)]
    cmds += ["pc=0x%x" % k0(CODE), "step %d" % lens[0]]
    for (ops, pr), n in zip(phases, lens[1:]):
        cmds.append("step %d" % n)
        if pr is not None:
            #  #530 repair 6: a phase may print SEVERAL registers; a bare string is unchanged.
            for _r in ((pr,) if isinstance(pr, str) else tuple(pr)):
                cmds.append("print %s" % _r)
    return cmds

def run(tag, consts, phases, want_regs, settle=3.0, spin=4.0):
    """want_regs: registers whose `print' MUST be present for the run to be READY.  A run that is
    not ready timed out rather than measured, and is retried ONCE with tripled patience -- the
    machine may be loaded.  Returns (txt, alive, retried)."""
    cmds = build(consts, phases)
    txt, alive = session(cmds, settle, spin)
    retried = False
    def _ready(t):
        #  #530 repair 6: an entry may be ("t3", 9) -- at least NINE prints of t3.  A bare string keeps the
        #  old meaning.  Without this a multi-phase session that times out HALFWAY still reports READY,
        #  because its FIRST print did arrive, and the rows then score against missing data.
        for w in want_regs:
            if isinstance(w, str):
                if printed(t, w) is None:
                    return False
            elif n_prints(t, w[0]) < w[1]:
                return False
        return True
    if not _ready(txt):
        retried = True
        txt, alive = session(cmds, settle * 3, spin * 3)
    if RAW:
        with open(os.path.join(RAW, "raw_%s.txt" % tag), "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write("### cmds\n" + "\n".join(cmds) + "\n### transcript\n" + txt)
    return txt, alive, retried

def evidence(txt, tag):
    for _l in txt.split(chr(10)):
        if ("lca_isa access function" in _l or "lca:" in _l or "pckbc:" in _l
                or "non-existant" in _l):
            print("  EVIDENCE[%s]: %s" % (tag, _l.strip()))

def hx(v):
    return "absent" if v is None else "0x%x" % v

ATTRIB = "in lca_isa access function"     # unique in src/; the arm's own diagnostic

print("lca_isalane_probe -- binary=%s label=%s machine=%s" % (BIN, LABEL, MACH))
print("  sparse LCA_PCI_SIO=0x%x  flat LCA_ISA_BASE=0x%x  ofs = (r>>5) & 3 = port & 3"
      % (LCA_PCI_SIO, LCA_ISA_BASE))
print("%-14s %-24s %-24s %-56s %s" % ("row", "got", "want", "note", ""))

# ================================================================ SESSION 1: the surviving rows
#  [A] STB 0xAA -> sparse(0x64)      8042 command, ofs 0
#  [B] LDBU     <- sparse(0x60)      8042 data,    ofs 0   -> 0x55            `live'
#  [C] LDBU     <- flat(0x3fd)       LSR via the internal mapping             `ofs1 devsig'
#  [D] LDBU     <- sparse(0x3fd)     LSR, len 1, ofs 1 -> 0 on HEAD           `ofs1 zero'
#  [E] LDL      <- sparse(0x3fd)     LSR, len 4, ofs 1 -> byte in lane 1      `ofs1 wide'
#  [F] LDBU     <- sparse(0x300)     no device, ofs 0                         `ctl'
C1 = [k0(SP(KBC_CMD)), KBC_SELFTEST, k0(SP(KBC_DAT)), k0(FLAT(UART_LSR)),
      k0(SP(UART_LSR)), k0(SP(NODEV_PORT))]
P1 = [([LDQ(T1, 0, T0), LDQ(T2, 8, T0), STB(T2, 0, T1),
        LDQ(T1, 16, T0)] + sent(T3) + [LDBU(T3, 0, T1)], "t3"),
      ([LDQ(T1, 24, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T1)], "t4"),
      ([LDQ(T1, 32, T0)] + sent(T5) + [LDBU(T5, 0, T1)], "t5"),
      ([LDQ(T1, 32, T0)] + sent(T6) + [LDL (T6, 0, T1)], "t6"),
      ([LDQ(T1, 40, T0)] + sent(T2) + [LDBU(T2, 0, T1)], "t2")]
t1x, a1, r1 = run("base", C1, P1, ["t3", "t4", "t5", "t6", "t2"])
b_live, b_flat, b_byte, b_wide, b_ctl = (printed(t1x, r) for r in ("t3", "t4", "t5", "t6", "t2"))
print("  RAW[base]: live=%s flatLSR=%s sparseLSR=%s wideLSR=%s nodev=%s alive=%s retried=%s"
      % (hx(b_live), hx(b_flat), hx(b_byte), hx(b_wide), hx(b_ctl), a1, r1))

row("base alive", "alive" if a1 else "dead", "alive",
    "CONTROL: nothing in this session may end the host")

#  ---- LIVENESS + DEVICE SIGNATURE, through the handler under test ---------------------------
#  Only the 8042 answers 0x55 to a load from ISA 0x60 after 0xAA reached ISA 0x64.  RAM and
#  unmapped space cannot, and this proves the SPARSE window reaches devices at all.
row("live", hx(b_live), hx(KBC_REPLY),
    "LIVENESS+DEVSIG: 8042 self-test reply, THROUGH lca_isa at ofs 0")

#  ---- the device byte exists, taken through the OTHER mapping --------------------------------
row("ofs1 devsig", hx(None if b_flat is None else b_flat & LSR_FIXED), hx(LSR_FIXED),
    "DEVSIG: TXRDY|TSRE are set by the ns16550 handler on every access")

#  ---- THE DEFECT ROW -------------------------------------------------------------------------
#  ORACLE POLARITY is the witness ladder's: assert the CORRECT behaviour, so this is RED pre-fix
#  and GREEN post-fix with the SAME script.  WANT = THE DEVICE BYTE.  For len == 1 the caller's
#  buffer has exactly one lane and it is data[0]; `i == ofs' with ofs 1 selects nothing, so the
#  byte is dropped.  (A DECLINE is the other defensible disposition and would also red this row --
#  which is correct: a decline must not be reported as a successful read of zero.  What a fix may
#  NOT do is keep answering a false zero.)
row("ofs1 zero", hx(None if b_byte is None else b_byte & LSR_FIXED),
    hx(None if b_flat is None else b_flat & LSR_FIXED),
    "*** DEFECT: byte read of ISA 0x3fd (LSR) -- port 1 mod 4 -> ofs 1 ***")

#  ---- the zero is an ANSWER, not a fault -----------------------------------------------------
#  A load whose memory_rw fails returns before writeback (cpu_alpha_instr_loadstore.c), leaving
#  the sentinel.  This row says which of the two happened, and on HEAD it is a FALSE SUCCESS.
row("ofs1 answer", "completed" if (b_byte is not None and b_byte != SENT) else "faulted/absent",
    "completed",
    "the handler returned 1: the guest was ANSWERED zero, not refused")

#  ---- the byte DOES reach the packing loop ---------------------------------------------------
#  Same address, same process, len 4 instead of 1: ofs 1 <= 4, so the loop writes data[1] and the
#  guest sees the byte shifted into lane 1.  This is what makes `ofs1 zero' a lane-packing finding
#  rather than "the sparse window does not reach the UART".
row("ofs1 wide", hx(None if b_wide is None else (b_wide >> 8) & LSR_FIXED), hx(LSR_FIXED),
    "SAME ADDRESS at len 4: the device byte lands in lane 1 -- so it arrived")

#  ---- the negative control for the window ----------------------------------------------------
row("ctl", "alive" if a1 else "dead", "alive",
    "CONTROL: byte access to an ISA port with NO device, ofs 0, survives")
row("ctl zero", hx(b_ctl), hx(0),
    "CONTROL: and it answers zero -- so zero alone proves nothing (see ofs1 devsig)")
evidence(t1x, "base")
print("")

# ================================================================ SESSION 2: ofs 2 ends the host
#  [A] LDBU <- flat(0x3fe)     MSR through the internal mapping   -> 0xb0
#  [B] LDL  <- sparse(0x3fe)   MSR, len 4, ofs 2 -> byte in lane 2, SURVIVES
#  [C] LDBU <- sparse(0x3fe)   MSR, len 1, ofs 2 -> 2 > 1 -> exit(1)
C2 = [k0(FLAT(UART_MSR)), k0(SP(UART_MSR))]
P2 = [([LDQ(T1, 0, T0)] + sent(T2) + [LDBU(T2, 0, T1)], "t2"),
      ([LDQ(T1, 8, T0)] + sent(T3) + [LDL (T3, 0, T1)], "t3"),
      ([LDQ(T1, 8, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T1)], None)]
t2x, a2, r2 = run("ofs2", C2, P2, ["t2", "t3"])
m_flat, m_wide = printed(t2x, "t2"), printed(t2x, "t3")
print("  RAW[ofs2]: flatMSR=%s wideMSR=%s alive=%s retried=%s" % (hx(m_flat), hx(m_wide), a2, r2))

row("ofs2 devsig", hx(m_flat), hx(MSR_FIXED),
    "DEVSIG: DCD|DSR|CTS are set by the ns16550 handler on every access")
row("ofs2 wide", hx(None if m_wide is None else (m_wide >> 16) & 0xff), hx(MSR_FIXED),
    "CONTROL: the SAME sparse address at len 4 survives and packs lane 2")
row("ofs2 exit", "alive" if a2 else "dead", "alive",
    "*** DEFECT: byte read of ISA 0x3fe (MSR) -- port 2 mod 4 -> ofs 2 ***")
row("ofs2 attrib", "yes" if (a2 or ATTRIB in t2x) else "died-elsewhere", "yes",
    "ATTRIBUTION: a death here must carry the arm's own diagnostic")
evidence(t2x, "ofs2")
print("")

# ================================================================ SESSION 3: ofs 3 ends the host
#  [A] STB 0x5a -> flat(0x3ff);  LDBU <- flat(0x3ff)   scratch register, through the flat mapping
#  [B] LDL  <- sparse(0x3ff)     len 4, ofs 3 -> byte in lane 3, SURVIVES
#  [C] LDBU <- sparse(0x3ff)     len 1, ofs 3 -> 3 > 1 -> exit(1)
C3 = [k0(FLAT(UART_SCR)), SCR_VALUE, k0(SP(UART_SCR))]
P3 = [([LDQ(T1, 0, T0), LDQ(T2, 8, T0), STB(T2, 0, T1)] + sent(T3) + [LDBU(T3, 0, T1)], "t3"),
      ([LDQ(T1, 16, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDL (T4, 0, T1)], "t4"),
      ([LDQ(T1, 16, T0)] + sent(T5) + [LDBU(T5, 0, T1)], None)]
t3x, a3, r3 = run("ofs3", C3, P3, ["t3", "t4"])
s_flat, s_wide = printed(t3x, "t3"), printed(t3x, "t4")
print("  RAW[ofs3]: flatSCR=%s wideSCR=%s alive=%s retried=%s" % (hx(s_flat), hx(s_wide), a3, r3))

row("ofs3 devsig", hx(s_flat), hx(SCR_VALUE),
    "DEVSIG: the ns16550 scratchpad returns the byte just written to it")
row("ofs3 wide", hx(None if s_wide is None else (s_wide >> 24) & 0xff), hx(SCR_VALUE),
    "CONTROL: the SAME sparse address at len 4 survives and packs lane 3")
row("ofs3 exit", "alive" if a3 else "dead", "alive",
    "*** DEFECT: byte read of ISA 0x3ff (scratch) -- port 3 mod 4 -> ofs 3 ***")
row("ofs3 attrib", "yes" if (a3 or ATTRIB in t3x) else "died-elsewhere", "yes",
    "ATTRIBUTION: a death here must carry the arm's own diagnostic")
evidence(t3x, "ofs3")
print("")

# ================================================================ #530: THE DETECTOR SESSIONS
#  Everything above is the rung-3 WITNESS, unchanged except for the sentinel.  Everything below is
#  the DETECTOR for the #530 fix (disposition B, FABLE_F2_RULING_20260914.md): the byte for port P
#  sits at buffer position `(P & 3) % len' on BOTH arms -- the rule HEAD already ran at every served
#  cell -- so no access ends the host and none is answered with a discarded byte.  Each row names
#  the enumerated mutant it kills (R3's table) and the channel: a FULL-REGISTER read (so a declined
#  load, which keeps the sentinel, and a replicated or mis-laned byte all differ from the want), or
#  a store read back through the FLAT mapping, which is a plain forward independent of the handler.
#
#  Ports: LSR 0x3fd (1 mod 4; TXRDY|TSRE forced), MSR 0x3fe (2 mod 4; reads reg|0xb0), IER 0x3f9
#  (1 mod 4; stores and returns the byte), SCR 0x3ff (3 mod 4; scratch).
UART_IER = 0x3f9
UART_MCR = 0x3fc              # 0 mod 4: modem control, stores and returns its low bits (pass 2)
DRETRIED = []

def prints_in_order(txt):
    #  The detector sessions reuse register names, so values are taken IN PRINT ORDER rather than
    #  by name (printed() returns the FIRST match, which would read an earlier phase's value).
    return [int(v, 16) for _, v in re.findall(r"print\s+(t\d)\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)", txt)]

def dsession(tag, consts, phases, names, settle=3.0, spin=4.0):
    """Returns (alive, {name: value|None}).  A run that did not produce every print is retried ONCE
    with tripled patience -- the load-tolerance rule the witness sessions already follow."""
    need = sum(1 for _, pr in phases if pr is not None)
    cmds = build(consts, phases)
    txt, alive = session(cmds, settle, spin)
    if len(prints_in_order(txt)) < need and alive:
        #  recorded since pass 2 (Opus): before it, this retry was SILENT and LCAISALANE_RETRIED reported
        #  only the three witness sessions
        DRETRIED.append(tag)
        txt, alive = session(cmds, settle * 3, spin * 3)
    if RAW:
        with open(os.path.join(RAW, "raw_%s.txt" % tag), "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write("### cmds\n" + "\n".join(cmds) + "\n### transcript\n" + txt)
    vals = prints_in_order(txt)
    got = {n: (vals[i] if i < len(vals) else None) for i, n in enumerate(names)}
    print("  RAW[%s]: alive=%s %s" % (tag, alive, " ".join("%s=%s" % (n, hx(got[n])) for n in names)))
    evidence(txt, tag)
    return alive, got

#  ---- SESSION 4: the cells that never ended the host (no mutant in the table can kill them) --
#  slots: 0 SP(LSR) 1 FLAT(LSR) 2 SP(MSR) 3 FLAT(MSR) 4 SP(IER) 5 FLAT(IER)
C4 = [k0(SP(UART_LSR)), k0(FLAT(UART_LSR)), k0(SP(UART_MSR)), k0(FLAT(UART_MSR)),
      k0(SP(UART_IER)), k0(FLAT(UART_IER))]
P4 = [([LDQ(T1, 8, T0)]  + sent(T2) + [LDBU(T2, 0, T1)], "t2"),                 # lsr  (flat)
      ([LDQ(T1, 0, T0)]  + sent(T3) + [LDBU(T3, 0, T1)], "t3"),                 # r11  len 1 ofs 1
      ([LDQ(T1, 0, T0)]  + sent(T4) + [LDWU(T4, 0, T1)], "t4"),                 # r21  len 2 ofs 1
      ([LDQ(T1, 0, T0)]  + sent(T5) + [LDQ (T5, 0, T1)], "t5"),                 # r81  len 8, P%8 == 5
      ([LDQ(T1, 24, T0)] + sent(T6) + [LDBU(T6, 0, T1)], "t6"),                 # msr  (flat)
      ([LDQ(T1, 16, T0)] + sent(T2) + [LDWU(T2, 0, T1)], "t2"),                 # r22  len 2 ofs 2
      (load_const(T3, 0x3a) + [LDQ(T1, 32, T0), STB(T3, 0, T1),
       LDQ(T1, 40, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T1)], "t4"),                  # w11  STB -> IER
      (load_const(T3, 0x5a00) + [LDQ(T1, 32, T0), STW(T3, 0, T1),
       LDQ(T1, 40, T0)] + sent(T5) + [LDBU(T5, 0, T1)], "t5"),                  # w21  STW -> IER
      (load_const(T3, 0x0040) + [LDQ(T1, 16, T0), STW(T3, 0, T1),
       LDQ(T1, 24, T0)] + sent(T6) + [LDBU(T6, 0, T1)], "t6")]                  # w22  STW -> MSR
a4, g4 = dsession("fold", C4, P4, ["lsr", "r11", "r21", "r81", "msr", "r22", "w11", "w21", "w22"])
lsr = g4["lsr"]
row("fold alive", "alive" if a4 else "dead", "alive",
    "CONTROL: none of these cells ever ended the host")
row("r11 byte", hx(g4["r11"]), hx(lsr),
    "(1,1) LDBU LSR, FULL register == the flat LSR: kills the A decline (sentinel) + C/HEAD (0)")
row("r21 anchor", hx(g4["r21"]), hx(None if lsr is None else lsr << 8),
    "(2,1) LDWU LSR: position 1 on HEAD and after -- the P mod len ANCHOR; kills replicate/lane edits")
row("r81 lane", hx(g4["r81"]), hx(None if lsr is None else lsr << 8),
    "(8,1) LDQ LSR, port 5 mod 8: lane 1, not 5 -- kills dropping the `& 3' (m_nomask)")
row("r22 byte", hx(g4["r22"]), hx(g4["msr"]),
    "(2,2) LDWU MSR == flat MSR at position 0: kills the A decline and HEAD's discarded byte")
row("w11 lands", hx(g4["w11"]), hx(0x3a),
    "(1,1) STB 0x3a -> IER, flat readback: lands on HEAD -- kills the A decline (F3)")
row("w21 lane", hx(g4["w21"]), hx(0x5a),
    "(2,1) STW 0x5a00 -> IER forwards position 1 on HEAD and after")
row("w22 lands", hx(g4["w22"]), hx(0x40 | MSR_FIXED),
    "(2,2) STW 0x0040 -> MSR, flat readback reg|0xb0: lands on HEAD -- kills the A decline")
print("")

#  ---- SESSION 4b (#530 pass 2): len 4 and 8 STORES, a wide access at a 0-mod-4 port, full-width reads ----
#  The Opus pass-2 seat BUILT ten mutants that scored 37/37 on the rows above, nine of them changing a guest
#  outcome (regress/artifacts/lcaisalane/opus_p2_20260914/): no row ever STORED at len 4 or 8 -- the canonical
#  real-OS `stl' outb -- no row made a wide access at a 0-mod-4 port, and no row read a whole len-4 value.  Every
#  cell here is served on HEAD and on the fix alike (none ends the host), so they share one process.
#  slots: 0 FLAT(SCR) 1 SP(SCR) 2 FLAT(MCR) 3 SP(MCR) 4 SP(LSR) 5 FLAT(LSR)
C4b = [k0(FLAT(UART_SCR)), k0(SP(UART_SCR)), k0(FLAT(UART_MCR)), k0(SP(UART_MCR)),
       k0(SP(UART_LSR)), k0(FLAT(UART_LSR))]
P4b = [(load_const(T3, 0x11) + [LDQ(T1, 0, T0), STB(T3, 0, T1)] + load_const(T3, 0x7e000000)
        + [LDQ(T1, 8, T0), STL(T3, 0, T1), LDQ(T1, 0, T0)] + sent(T2) + [LDBU(T2, 0, T1)], "t2"),   # (4,3) W
       (load_const(T3, 0x6d000000) + [LDQ(T1, 8, T0), STQ(T3, 0, T1), LDQ(T1, 0, T0)]
        + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T1)], "t4"),                                                      # (8,3) W
       (load_const(T3, 0x01) + [LDQ(T1, 16, T0), STB(T3, 0, T1)] + load_const(T3, 0x03)
        + [LDQ(T1, 24, T0), STL(T3, 0, T1), LDQ(T1, 16, T0)] + sent(T5) + [LDBU(T5, 0, T1)], "t5"),  # (4,0) W
       ([LDQ(T1, 24, T0)] + sent(T6) + [LDL(T6, 0, T1)], "t6"),                                     # (4,0) R
       ([LDQ(T1, 32, T0)] + sent(T2) + [LDL(T2, 0, T1)], "t2"),                                     # (4,1) R full
       ([LDQ(T1, 8, T0)]  + sent(T3) + [LDQ(T3, 0, T1)], "t3"),                                     # (8,3) R
       ([LDQ(T1, 40, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T1)], "t4")]                                    # lsr (flat)
a4b, g4b = dsession("wide", C4b, P4b, ["st4", "st8", "mcr4", "mcrrd", "ld4", "ld8", "lsr"])
lsrb = g4b["lsr"]
row("wide alive", "alive" if a4b else "dead", "alive",
    "CONTROL: every access in this session is served on HEAD and on the fix")
row("st4 lane3", hx(g4b["st4"]), hx(0x7e),
    "(4,3) STL 0x7e000000 -> SCR, flat readback: kills p_wbit1, p_wnarrow, p_wdrop4, p_waddr4 (opus p2)")
row("st8 lane3", hx(g4b["st8"]), hx(0x6d),
    "(8,3) STQ 0x6d000000 -> SCR, flat readback: kills p_q8lane and p_wbit1")
row("st4 lane0", hx(g4b["mcr4"]), hx(0x03),
    "(4,0) STL 3 -> MCR (0 mod 4), flat readback: kills p_lane0decl (the store dropped)")
row("ld4 lane0", hx(g4b["mcrrd"]), hx(0x03),
    "(4,0) LDL MCR, FULL register: kills p_lane0decl (the load declined, sentinel kept)")
row("ld4 full", hx(g4b["ld4"]), hx(None if lsrb is None else lsrb << 8),
    "(4,1) LDL LSR, FULL register == lsr << 8: kills p_rep4 (byte replicated at len 4)")
row("ld8 lane3", hx(g4b["ld8"]), hx(0x6d000000),
    "(8,3) LDQ SCR, FULL register: kills p_q8lane's read side")
print("")

#  ---- SESSION 4c (#530 pass 2): the 1 KB SPLIT heads -- MEASURABLE on K0SEG after all --------------
#  The table-1 contract disclosed the split heads as needing a TRANSLATED access.  FALSE (Opus pass 2, measured):
#  MEMORY_RW's split is keyed on `!(misc_flags & PHYSICAL) && cpu->translate_v2p != NULL', Alpha always sets
#  alpha_translate_v2p, and the load/store path passes only CACHE_DATA without an alignment check -- so an
#  unaligned LDQ/LDL on K0SEG splits at port 0x3ff/0x400.  SP(SCR) + 0x1d is 3 bytes below the 1 KB line: an LDQ
#  there hands the handler a len-3 head at ofs 3 -- the (3,3) cell the fix CHANGED (HEAD discarded the byte).
#  The tail goes to flat port 0x400, where nothing is mapped (one `non-existant' line per access, expected).
#  slots: 0 FLAT(SCR) 1 0x5a 2 SP(SCR)+0x1d 3 SP(SCR)+0x1b 4 SP(SCR)
C4c = [k0(FLAT(UART_SCR)), SCR_VALUE, k0(SP(UART_SCR) + 0x1d), k0(SP(UART_SCR) + 0x1b), k0(SP(UART_SCR))]
P4c = [([LDQ(T1, 0, T0), LDQ(T2, 8, T0), STB(T2, 0, T1)] + sent(T3) + [LDBU(T3, 0, T1)], "t3"),   # scr (flat)
       ([LDQ(T1, 16, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDQ(T4, 0, T1)], "t4"),                                  # LDQ head len 3
       ([LDQ(T1, 16, T0)] + sent(T5) + [LDL(T5, 0, T1)], "t5"),                                  # LDL head len 3
       ([LDQ(T1, 24, T0)] + sent(T6) + [LDQ(T6, 0, T1)], "t6"),                                  # LDQ head len 5
       ([LDQ(T1, 32, T0)] + sent(T2) + [LDQ(T2, 0, T1)], "t2"),                                  # aligned control
       (load_const(T3, 0x00c3007e) + [LDQ(T1, 16, T0), STQ(T3, 0, T1), LDQ(T1, 0, T0)]
        + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T1)], "t4")]                                                   # STQ head len 3
a4c, g4c = dsession("split", C4c, P4c, ["scr", "q3", "l3", "q5", "qal", "w3"])
row("split alive", "alive" if a4c else "dead", "alive",
    "CONTROL: no split head here ends the host on HEAD or on the fix")
row("split devsig", hx(g4c["scr"]), hx(SCR_VALUE),
    "DEVSIG: the scratchpad holds 0x5a through the flat mapping")
row("h3 ldq", hx(g4c["q3"]), hx(SCR_VALUE),
    "(3,3) LDQ head: position 3 % 3 = 0 -- RED ON HEAD (discarded byte); kills & (len-1) (0x5a0000)")
row("h3 ldl", hx(g4c["l3"]), hx(SCR_VALUE),
    "(3,3) LDL head: the no-BWX route to the fix-changed cell -- RED ON HEAD; kills & (len-1)")
row("h5 ldq", hx(g4c["q5"]), hx(SCR_VALUE << 24),
    "(5,3) LDQ head: position 3 % 5 = 3; kills & (len-1) (position 3 & 4 = 0)")
row("split al", hx(g4c["qal"]), hx(SCR_VALUE << 24),
    "CONTROL: the aligned LDQ at the same port packs lane 3")
row("h3 stq", hx(g4c["w3"]), hx(0x7e),
    "(3,3) STQ 0x00c3007e head: position 0 forwards 0x7e; kills & (len-1) (position 2 = 0xc3)")
print("")

#  ---- SESSION 4d (#530 closing pass 1): THE WHOLE len x lane x direction CROSS for the direct widths ------------
#  Closing pass 1 found the same class as pass 2 one cell over -- codex an LDQ at lane 0, grok (2,0) and (8,0), minimax a
#  wide STORE at lane 1, glm one at lane 2 -- and the PRETABLE's sibling-parity line had checked WIDTHS per arm and said
#  nothing about LANES per width, which is how it passed.  Two passes, one class: this repair rows the whole cross.  The
#  direct widths are 1, 2, 4, 8 and the lane is P & 3; every (len, lane, dir) cell is now rowed somewhere -- the ones
#  below are the nine no earlier session reached, plus FULL-register len-4 reads at lanes 2 and 3 (the Opus
#  closing seat, measured: `ofs2/ofs3 wide' mask one lane, so a mis-fill of the OTHER positions scored 51/51).  Lane 0 = MCR 0x3fc, lane 1 = IER 0x3f9, lane 2 = MSR 0x3fe (a store
#  is kept; a read returns reg | 0xb0), lane 3 = SCR 0x3ff.  None of these cells ever ended the host.
#  slots: 0 FLAT(MCR) 1 SP(MCR) 2 FLAT(IER) 3 SP(IER) 4 FLAT(MSR) 5 SP(MSR) 6 FLAT(SCR) 7 0x5a 8 SP(SCR)
C4d = [k0(FLAT(UART_MCR)), k0(SP(UART_MCR)), k0(FLAT(UART_IER)), k0(SP(UART_IER)),
       k0(FLAT(UART_MSR)), k0(SP(UART_MSR)), k0(FLAT(UART_SCR)), SCR_VALUE, k0(SP(UART_SCR))]
P4d = [(load_const(T3, 0x03) + [LDQ(T1, 0, T0), STB(T3, 0, T1),                                  # MCR = 3 (flat)
        LDQ(T1, 8, T0)] + sent(T2) + [LDWU(T2, 0, T1)], "t2"),                                    # R(2,0)
       ([LDQ(T1, 8, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDQ(T4, 0, T1)], "t4"),                                     # R(8,0)
       ([LDQ(T1, 40, T0)] + sent(T5) + [LDQ(T5, 0, T1)], "t5"),                                    # R(8,2)
       ([LDQ(T1, 32, T0)] + sent(T6) + [LDBU(T6, 0, T1)], "t6"),                                   # msr (flat)
       ([LDQ(T1, 40, T0)] + sent(T2) + [LDL(T2, 0, T1)], "t2"),                                    # R(4,2) FULL
       ([LDQ(T1, 48, T0), LDQ(T2, 56, T0), STB(T2, 0, T1),
         LDQ(T1, 64, T0)] + sent(T3) + [LDL(T3, 0, T1)], "t3"),                                   # R(4,3) FULL
       (load_const(T3, 0x0002) + [LDQ(T1, 8, T0), STW(T3, 0, T1),
        LDQ(T1, 0, T0)] + sent(T2) + [LDBU(T2, 0, T1)], "t2"),                                     # W(2,0) -> MCR
       (load_const(T3, 0x01) + [LDQ(T1, 8, T0), STQ(T3, 0, T1),
        LDQ(T1, 0, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T1)], "t4"),                                     # W(8,0) -> MCR
       (load_const(T3, 0x4d00) + [LDQ(T1, 24, T0), STL(T3, 0, T1),
        LDQ(T1, 16, T0)] + sent(T5) + [LDBU(T5, 0, T1)], "t5"),                                    # W(4,1) -> IER
       (load_const(T3, 0x5c00) + [LDQ(T1, 24, T0), STQ(T3, 0, T1),
        LDQ(T1, 16, T0)] + sent(T6) + [LDBU(T6, 0, T1)], "t6"),                                    # W(8,1) -> IER
       (load_const(T3, 0x00400000) + [LDQ(T1, 40, T0), STQ(T3, 0, T1),
        LDQ(T1, 32, T0)] + sent(T2) + [LDBU(T2, 0, T1)], "t2"),                                    # W(8,2) -> MSR
       ([LDQ(T1, 32, T0), STB(ZERO, 0, T1)]                                                        # MSR = 0 (flat)
        + load_const(T3, 0x00040000) + [LDQ(T1, 40, T0), STL(T3, 0, T1),
        LDQ(T1, 32, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T1)], "t4")]                                    # W(4,2) -> MSR
a4d, g4d = dsession("cross", C4d, P4d, ["r20", "r80", "r82", "msr", "r42", "r43", "w20", "w80", "w41", "w81", "w82",
                                          "w42"])
row("cross alive", "alive" if a4d else "dead", "alive", "CONTROL: none of these cells ever ended the host")
row("r20 byte", hx(g4d["r20"]), hx(0x03), "(2,0) LDWU MCR: position 0")
row("r80 byte", hx(g4d["r80"]), hx(0x03), "(8,0) LDQ MCR: position 0 -- codex's `return len != 8 || ofs'")
row("r82 lane", hx(g4d["r82"]), hx(None if g4d["msr"] is None else g4d["msr"] << 16), "(8,2) LDQ MSR: position 2")
row("r42 full", hx(g4d["r42"]), hx(None if g4d["msr"] is None else g4d["msr"] << 16),
    "(4,2) LDL MSR, FULL register: position 2 and the other positions 0 (Opus c1_r4)")
row("r43 full", hx(g4d["r43"]), hx(SCR_VALUE << 24), "(4,3) LDL SCR, FULL register: position 3, the rest 0")
row("w20 lands", hx(g4d["w20"]), hx(0x02), "(2,0) STW 0x0002 -> MCR, flat readback")
row("w80 lands", hx(g4d["w80"]), hx(0x01), "(8,0) STQ 0x01 -> MCR, flat readback")
row("w41 lane", hx(g4d["w41"]), hx(0x4d), "(4,1) STL 0x4d00 -> IER: position 1 (minimax)")
row("w81 lane", hx(g4d["w81"]), hx(0x5c), "(8,1) STQ 0x5c00 -> IER: position 1")
row("w82 lane", hx(g4d["w82"]), hx(0x40 | MSR_FIXED), "(8,2) STQ 0x400000 -> MSR: position 2 (glm's store side)")
row("w42 lane", hx(g4d["w42"]), hx(0x04 | MSR_FIXED), "(4,2) STL 0x40000 -> MSR: position 2")
print("")

#  ---- SESSIONS 4e (#530 closing pass 1): the SPLIT heads at every length 1..7, both arms --------------------------
#  A split head always ends at a port 0x1f mod 0x20 (lane 3 -- the lane is the invariant; this probe's boundary is port
#  0x3ff), so its cells are (len, 3) for len 1..7.  4c rowed len 3 (R, W) and
#  5 (R); deepseek found `len == 6' free.  Heads of len 6 and 7 (R) and 5-7 (W) never ended the host and share one
#  process; heads of len 1 and 2 DID (they are the (1,3) / (2,3) cells reached WITHOUT BWX -- `lcaisaunaligned'), so each
#  gets its own process with an `alive' row, like sessions 5-10.
#  STQ value 0xc3a5967e: position 0 = 0x7e, 1 = 0x96, 3 = 0xc3.
SPLITV = 0xc3a5967e
C4e = [k0(FLAT(UART_SCR)), SCR_VALUE, k0(SP(UART_SCR) + 0x1a), k0(SP(UART_SCR) + 0x19), k0(SP(UART_SCR) + 0x1b)]
P4e = [([LDQ(T1, 0, T0), LDQ(T2, 8, T0), STB(T2, 0, T1)] + sent(T3) + [LDBU(T3, 0, T1)], "t3"),  # scr = 0x5a (flat)
       ([LDQ(T1, 16, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDQ(T4, 0, T1)], "t4"),                                  # R head 6
       ([LDQ(T1, 24, T0)] + sent(T5) + [LDQ(T5, 0, T1)], "t5"),                                  # R head 7
       (load_const(T3, SPLITV) + [LDQ(T1, 32, T0), STQ(T3, 0, T1),
        LDQ(T1, 0, T0)] + sent(T6) + [LDBU(T6, 0, T1)], "t6"),                                   # W head 5 -> SCR
       ([LDQ(T1, 0, T0), STB(ZERO, 0, T1)] + load_const(T3, SPLITV) + [LDQ(T1, 16, T0), STQ(T3, 0, T1),
        LDQ(T1, 0, T0)] + sent(T2) + [LDBU(T2, 0, T1)], "t2"),                                   # W head 6 -> SCR
       ([LDQ(T1, 0, T0), STB(ZERO, 0, T1)] + load_const(T3, SPLITV) + [LDQ(T1, 24, T0), STQ(T3, 0, T1),
        LDQ(T1, 0, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T1)], "t4")]                                   # W head 7 -> SCR
a4e, g4e = dsession("split67", C4e, P4e, ["scr", "q6", "q7", "w5", "w6", "w7"])
row("split67 alive", "alive" if a4e else "dead", "alive", "CONTROL: heads of len 5-7 never ended the host")
row("h6 ldq", hx(g4e["q6"]), hx(SCR_VALUE << 24), "(6,3) LDQ head: position 3 -- deepseek's `len == 6'")
row("h7 ldq", hx(g4e["q7"]), hx(SCR_VALUE << 24), "(7,3) LDQ head: position 3")
row("h5 stq", hx(g4e["w5"]), hx(0xc3), "(5,3) STQ head: position 3 forwards 0xc3")
row("h6 stq", hx(g4e["w6"]), hx(0xc3), "(6,3) STQ head: position 3")
row("h7 stq", hx(g4e["w7"]), hx(0xc3), "(7,3) STQ head: position 3")
print("")

for tag, off, store, wantf, note in (
        ("h1 ldq", 0x1f, False, lambda: SCR_VALUE, "(1,3) LDQ head: was a host death on HEAD (no BWX)"),
        ("h2 ldq", 0x1e, False, lambda: SCR_VALUE << 8, "(2,3) LDQ head: position 1; was a host death on HEAD"),
        ("h1 stq", 0x1f, True, lambda: 0x7e, "(1,3) STQ head: position 0; was a host death on HEAD"),
        ("h2 stq", 0x1e, True, lambda: 0x96, "(2,3) STQ head: position 1; was a host death on HEAD")):
    C = [k0(FLAT(UART_SCR)), SCR_VALUE, k0(SP(UART_SCR) + off)]
    if store:
        P = [(load_const(T3, SPLITV) + [LDQ(T1, 16, T0), STQ(T3, 0, T1),
              LDQ(T1, 0, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T1)], "t4")]
    else:
        P = [([LDQ(T1, 0, T0), LDQ(T2, 8, T0), STB(T2, 0, T1),
               LDQ(T1, 16, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDQ(T4, 0, T1)], "t4")]
    ah, gh = dsession(tag.replace(" ", "_"), C, P, ["v"])
    row("%s alive" % tag, "alive" if ah else "dead", "alive", note)
    row(tag, hx(gh["v"]), hx(wantf()), note.split(":")[0] + " value")
print("")

#  ---- SESSIONS 4f (#530 closing pass 2): the split TAIL cells, at a MAPPED port ------------------------------------
#  A 1 KB split (MRW_SPLIT_MASK 0x3ff on the VIRTUAL address = 32 sparse ports) puts its head at a port 0x1f mod 0x20
#  (lane 3) and its TAIL at the next port, 0 mod 0x20 (lane 0), of length 8 - h (LDQ/STQ) or 4 - h (LDL/STL).  Tails of
#  length 1, 2 and 4 are handler-identical to the direct lane-0 cells; tails of length 3, 5, 6 and 7 exist ONLY as tails.
#  The split rows above put their tails on port 0x400, where nothing is mapped: a tail read there is zero-filled and a
#  tail write goes nowhere, so they see a tail DECLINE and never a tail MIS-PLACEMENT (principle 2 -- zero readback alone
#  proves nothing -- applied to the probe's own tails).  The Opus closing-pass-2 seat MEASURED
#  `if ((relative_addr & 3) == 0 && len > 2 && (len & 3)) ofs = 1;' at 77/77 (regress/artifacts/lcaisalane/
#  opus_c2_20260914/).  These two sessions are its discriminator, ported: an unaligned LDQ/STQ at SP(0x5f) + 0x1f / 0x1e
#  / 0x1d / 0x1b is a head of 1 / 2 / 3 / 5 at port 0x5f (unmapped) and a tail of 7 / 6 / 5 / 3 at port 0x60, the 8042
#  DATA port.  Read channel: the self-test reply 0x55 lands at register byte h.  Write channel: the 8042 command byte
