#  (0x60 to the command port, then the tail's forwarded byte; read back with 0x20 through the FLAT mapping).  The 1- and
#  2-byte heads at 0x5f are (1,3) and (2,3) cells, so on HEAD each session ends the host at its first split.
TAILHEADS = ((1, 0x1f), (2, 0x1e), (3, 0x1d), (5, 0x1b))   # (head length h, byte offset) -> tail 8 - h at port 0x60
C4f = [k0(FLAT(KBC_CMD)), KBC_SELFTEST, k0(SP(KBC_DAT))] + [k0(SP(0x5f) + o) for _, o in TAILHEADS]
def kbc_selftest(): return [LDQ(T1, 0, T0), LDQ(T2, 8, T0), STB(T2, 0, T1)]
P4f = [(kbc_selftest() + [LDQ(T1, 16, T0)] + sent(T3) + [LDQ(T3, 0, T1)], "t3")]                  # control: (8,0)
for i in range(len(TAILHEADS)):
    P4f.append((kbc_selftest() + [LDQ(T1, 24 + 8 * i, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDQ(T4, 0, T1)], "t4"))
a4f, g4f = dsession("tailr", C4f, P4f, ["ctl"] + ["t%d" % (8 - h) for h, _ in TAILHEADS])
row("tailr alive", "alive" if a4f else "dead", "alive", "CONTROL (1/2-byte heads at 0x5f were host deaths on HEAD)")
row("tailr ctl", hx(g4f["ctl"]), hx(KBC_REPLY), "CONTROL: aligned LDQ at SP(0x60), the (8,0) cell, reads 0x55")
for h, _ in TAILHEADS:
    row("tailr t%d" % (8 - h), hx(g4f["t%d" % (8 - h)]), hx(KBC_REPLY << (8 * h)),
        "(%d,0) LDQ TAIL at port 0x60: position 0 -> register byte %d" % (8 - h, h))
print("")

def tailv(h): return (0x44 << (8 * h)) | (0x21 << (8 * (h + 1)))   # tail position 0 = 0x44, position 1 = 0x21
C4g = ([k0(FLAT(KBC_CMD)), 0x60, 0x20, k0(FLAT(KBC_DAT))] + [k0(SP(0x5f) + o) for _, o in TAILHEADS]
       + [tailv(h) for h, _ in TAILHEADS])
def kbc_ldcmd(): return [LDQ(T1, 0, T0), LDQ(T2, 8, T0), STB(T2, 0, T1)]
def kbc_rdcmd(): return [LDQ(T1, 0, T0), LDQ(T2, 16, T0), STB(T2, 0, T1), LDQ(T1, 24, T0)]
def kbc_reset(): return kbc_ldcmd() + load_const(T3, 0x04) + [LDQ(T1, 24, T0), STB(T3, 0, T1)]   # cmdbyte = 0x04 (flat)
P4g = [(kbc_reset() + kbc_rdcmd() + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T1)], "t4")]                          # control: flat
#  Each tail phase RESETS the command byte to 0x04 first: without it a DECLINED tail store would read back the previous
#  phase's 0x44 and pass (the order is t7, t6, t5, t3), so these rows would see a mis-placement and never a decline.
for i in range(len(TAILHEADS)):
    P4g.append((kbc_reset() + kbc_ldcmd() + [LDQ(T3, 64 + 8 * i, T0), LDQ(T1, 32 + 8 * i, T0), STQ(T3, 0, T1)]
                + kbc_rdcmd() + sent(T5) + [LDBU(T5, 0, T1)], "t5"))
a4g, g4g = dsession("tailw", C4g, P4g, ["ctl"] + ["t%d" % (8 - h) for h, _ in TAILHEADS])
row("tailw alive", "alive" if a4g else "dead", "alive", "CONTROL (1/2-byte heads at 0x5f were host deaths on HEAD)")
row("tailw ctl", hx(g4g["ctl"]), hx(0x04), "CONTROL: flat command-byte round trip")
for h, _ in TAILHEADS:
    row("tailw t%d" % (8 - h), hx(g4g["t%d" % (8 - h)]), hx(0x44),
        "(%d,0) STQ TAIL at port 0x60 forwards position 0 (0x44), not 1 (0x21)" % (8 - h))
print("")

#  ---- SESSION 4h (#530 closing pass 2): a store to a port whose READ has a side effect ---------------------------
#  Every port the 77-row instrument stored to read back without a side effect, so deleting the write arm's `return' -- the
#  store then FALLS INTO the read arm, a phantom 1-byte read of the same port -- scored 77/77 (x_wfallthru, table 3 and
#  the Opus closing-pass-2 seat).  It is not equivalent for a guest: dev_ns16550.c's com_iir READ arm clears IIR_TXRDY
#  (and com_data's calls console_readchar, so every THR store would eat a pending input character).  Channel: arm
#  TXRDY (IER 0 -> ETXRDY), store 0 through lca_isa to ISA 0x3fa (FCR on a write, IIR on a read), flat IIR & 3.
UART_IIR = 0x3fa                    # 2 mod 4 -> lane 2
def iir_arm(): return load_const(T3, 0x02) + [LDQ(T1, 0, T0), STB(ZERO, 0, T1), STB(T3, 0, T1)]
C4h = [k0(FLAT(UART_IER)), k0(FLAT(UART_IIR)), k0(SP(UART_IIR))]
P4h = [(iir_arm() + [LDQ(T1, 8, T0)] + sent(T2) + [LDBU(T2, 0, T1)], "t2"),                     # control, no lca store
       (iir_arm() + [LDQ(T1, 16, T0), STB(ZERO, 0, T1), LDQ(T1, 8, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T1)], "t4"),  # (1,2)
       (iir_arm() + [LDQ(T1, 16, T0), STL(ZERO, 0, T1), LDQ(T1, 8, T0)] + sent(T5) + [LDBU(T5, 0, T1)], "t5")]  # (4,2)
a4h, g4h = dsession("iir", C4h, P4h, ["ctl", "stb", "stl"])
iir3 = lambda v: None if v is None else v & 0x03
#  *** VALUE / POSITION rows, NOT count rows.  ns16550 IIR is a ONE-SHOT LATCH (`dev_ns16550.c:211-213' -- the
#  read clears IIR_TXRDY once; the write path is `d->fcr = idata;'), so `TXRDY survives the store' is true of
#  ONE store and of TWO alike.  These rows pin WHICH BYTE reached the port; the forward COUNT at lane 2 is
#  carried by `pit2' (repair 6).  Measured: RWall 167/185 and WWall 169/185 moved NONE of them. ***
row("iir alive", "alive" if a4h else "dead", "alive", "CONTROL")
row("iir ctl", hx(iir3(g4h["ctl"])), hx(0x02), "CONTROL: IER 0 -> ETXRDY sets IIR_TXRDY; flat IIR & 3 = TXRDY")
row("iir stb", hx(iir3(g4h["stb"])), hx(0x02), "(1,2) STB 0 -> SP(0x3fa): TXRDY survives the store (x_wfallthru)")
row("iir stl", hx(iir3(g4h["stl"])), hx(0x02), "(4,2) STL 0 -> SP(0x3fa): TXRDY survives the store (x_wfallthru)")
print("")

#  ---- SESSIONS 4i-4k (#530 closing pass 3, REPAIR 4 -- the flagship ruling, option (c)) -------------------------
#  Closing pass 3 measured the same channel escaping GATED: `return' removed only for len 8 (W1) or len 2 (W2), for
#  every width but the two 4h stores at (W4), or only off lane 2 (W3) -- all 93/93 and guest-visible (the Opus seat,
#  regress/artifacts/lcaisalane/opus_c3_20260914/) -- and its read-arm mirror RD2 (the forward DUPLICATED) likewise.
#  The flagship ruled the contract's unscoped x_wfallthru entry a wrong record to be fixed in-round by rowing every
#  OBSERVABLE cell of the class (regress/artifacts/lcaisalane/FABLE_C3_RULING_20260914.md).  A phantom read shows only
#  where the forwarded-to port's READ has a side effect that is ARMED: lane 2 -> IIR 0x3fa (TXRDY armed, as 4h); lane 0
#  -> the 8042 data port 0x60 with a reply QUEUED (a self-test's 0x55, then 0xd1 so the store lands in output_byte, not
#  in the keyboard; a phantom read pops the 0x55 and the flat status's DIB bit drops).  The 8042 queue is a FIFO, so
#  each lane-0 width gets its own process (one queued reply each).  New sessions only: no earlier session changes.
P4i = [(iir_arm() + [LDQ(T1, 8, T0)] + sent(T2) + [LDBU(T2, 0, T1)], "t2"),                     # control, no lca store
       (iir_arm() + [LDQ(T1, 16, T0), STW(ZERO, 0, T1), LDQ(T1, 8, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T1)], "t4"),  # (2,2)
       (iir_arm() + [LDQ(T1, 16, T0), STQ(ZERO, 0, T1), LDQ(T1, 8, T0)] + sent(T5) + [LDBU(T5, 0, T1)], "t5")]  # (8,2)
a4i, g4i = dsession("iirw", C4h, P4i, ["ctl", "stw", "stq"])
row("iirw alive", "alive" if a4i else "dead", "alive", "CONTROL")
row("iirw ctl", hx(iir3(g4i["ctl"])), hx(0x02), "CONTROL: IER 0 -> ETXRDY sets IIR_TXRDY")
row("iir stw", hx(iir3(g4i["stw"])), hx(0x02), "(2,2) STW 0 -> SP(0x3fa): TXRDY survives (W1/W2/W4: gated fallthrough)")
row("iir stq", hx(iir3(g4i["stq"])), hx(0x02), "(8,2) STQ 0 -> SP(0x3fa): TXRDY survives (W1/W4)")
print("")

C4j = [k0(FLAT(UART_IER)), k0(SP(UART_IIR))]
P4j = [(iir_arm() + [LDQ(T1, 8, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T1)], "t4")]                     # (1,2) READ via lca
a4j, g4j = dsession("iirr", C4j, P4j, ["rd"])
row("iirr alive", "alive" if a4j else "dead", "alive", "CONTROL ((1,2) read -- a host death on HEAD)")
row("iir lca rd", hx(iir3(g4j["rd"])), hx(0x02), "(1,2) LDBU SP(0x3fa) after arming: TXRDY read ONCE (RD2: forwarded twice)")
print("")

KBC_WROUTPORT = 0xd1                # the next data-port write goes to output_byte, not to the keyboard
for w, stop in (("b", STB), ("w", STW), ("l", STL), ("q", STQ)):
    C = [k0(FLAT(KBC_CMD)), KBC_SELFTEST, KBC_WROUTPORT, k0(SP(KBC_DAT))]
    P = [([LDQ(T1, 0, T0), LDQ(T2, 8, T0), STB(T2, 0, T1), LDQ(T2, 16, T0), STB(T2, 0, T1)]
          + sent(T3) + [LDBU(T3, 0, T1)], "t3"),                                                # flat status, reply queued
         ([LDQ(T1, 24, T0), stop(ZERO, 0, T1), LDQ(T1, 0, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T1)], "t4")]  # lca store, status
    ak, gk = dsession("kbcq_" + w, C, P, ["pre", "post"])
    dib = lambda v: None if v is None else v & 0x01
    lenw = {"b": 1, "w": 2, "l": 4, "q": 8}[w]
    row("kbcq %s alive" % w, "alive" if ak else "dead", "alive", "CONTROL")
    row("kbcq %s pre" % w, hx(dib(gk["pre"])), hx(0x01), "CONTROL: the self-test reply is queued (status DIB)")
    row("kbcq %s dib" % w, hx(dib(gk["post"])), hx(0x01),
        "(%d,0) ST 0 -> SP(0x60): the queued reply survives the store (W3: lane-gated fallthrough)" % lenw)
print("")

#  ---- `kbcmd' (#530 repair 6): THE SAME 8042, A DIFFERENT ORACLE -- and the difference is the whole point. ------
#  `kbcq' above asks "does the queued reply SURVIVE the store?".  That is a LATCH question: it separates zero accesses
#  from at least one, and a DOUBLED store cannot move it, because two stores leave the reply just as queued as one.
#  `kbcmd' asks "did the command produce EXACTLY ONE reply?", which is a COUNT question and is what this class needs:
#      DIB=1 (a reply is queued)  ->  pop 0x55  ->  DIB=0 (the queue is now EMPTY).
#  A doubled store delivers 0xAA twice, so TWO replies queue and DIB is STILL 1 after the single pop.  A phantom read
#  (x_wfallthru) pops the reply early, so DIB is already 0 BEFORE the pop.  The two escapes move OPPOSITE rows.
#  The command goes through lca_isa at SP(0x64): 0x64 & 3 == 0, so ofs = 0 % len = 0 at every width, and a const of
#  0xAA puts the command byte at position 0 for all four.  One session per width -- the 8042 queue is FIFO and
#  stateful, which is why `kbcq' is built the same way.  No new device: this is the 8042 the tail rows already use.
for w, stop in (("b", STB), ("w", STW), ("l", STL), ("q", STQ)):
    lenw = {"b": 1, "w": 2, "l": 4, "q": 8}[w]
    C = [k0(SP(KBC_CMD)), KBC_SELFTEST, k0(FLAT(KBC_CMD)), k0(FLAT(KBC_DAT))]
    P = [([LDQ(T1, 0, T0), LDQ(T2, 8, T0), stop(T2, 0, T1), LDQ(T1, 16, T0)]
          + sent(T3) + [LDBU(T3, 0, T1)], "t3"),                       # command THROUGH lca, then flat status
         ([LDQ(T1, 24, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T1)], "t4"),     # pop the reply, flat
         ([LDQ(T1, 16, T0)] + sent(T5) + [LDBU(T5, 0, T1)], "t5")]     # flat status again: the queue must be EMPTY
    am, gm = dsession("kbcmd_" + w, C, P, ["pre", "pop", "post"])
    dibm = lambda v: None if v is None else v & 0x01
    row("kbcmd %s alive" % w, "alive" if am else "dead", "alive", "CONTROL")
    row("kbcmd %s pre" % w, hx(dibm(gm["pre"])), hx(0x01),
        "(%d,0) ST 0xAA -> SP(0x64) THROUGH lca: the command reached the 8042 and queued a reply" % lenw)
    row("kbcmd %s pop" % w, hx(gm["pop"]), hx(KBC_REPLY),
        "(%d,0) ...and the reply is the self-test 0x55 -- DEVICE SIGNATURE, not just a set bit" % lenw)
    row("kbcmd %s post" % w, hx(dibm(gm["post"])), hx(0x00),
        "(%d,0) *** THE COUNT ORACLE: after ONE pop the queue is EMPTY, so the command was delivered EXACTLY ONCE.  "
        "A DUPLICATED store queues two replies and leaves DIB=1 here -- MEASURED, WWall reds this row at all four "
        "widths.  *** THIS NOTE USED TO ADD `a PHANTOM read pops early and drops pre to 0; kbcq dib cannot see "
        "either'.  BOTH HALVES ARE MEASURED FALSE (#530 closing pass 6, the Opus seat): under Wpure every "
        "kbcmd pre stays 0x1, and kbcq dib goes 0x1 -> 0x0 at all four widths -- kbcq is the ONLY 8042 family "
        "that reds there. ***  The roles are INVERTED for that shape: kbcq is on 0x60 (DATA, a read POPS) and "
        "SEES the phantom read; kbcmd is on 0x64 (STATUS, a read is INERT) and CANNOT.  So this row kills the "
        "write arm's DUPLICATE -- which is exactly what PRETABLE's CHANNEL block said, narrowly and "
        "correctly -- and `kbcq %s dib' carries the phantom read." % (lenw, w))
print("")

#  ---- SESSIONS 4l-4q (#530 closing pass 4, REPAIR 5 -- the forward-count CLASS CLOSURE) --------------------------
#  Closing pass 4 found the class a THIRD consecutive time, each escape guest-visible at 111/111 (the Opus seat,
#  regress/artifacts/lcaisalane/opus_c4_20260914/): RD2 gated on the width (only (1,2) was rowed), the split-TAIL cells,
#  lane-0 reads, lane-3 direct.  The flagship ruling (regress/artifacts/lcaisalane/FABLE_C4_RULING_20260915.md, part B)
#  owes the enumeration DERIVED FROM THE HANDLER in one diff: its two arms (a store's phantom read after the forward; a
#  load's forward duplicated) x every width kind x every lane.  Each observable cell below has a row, on an ARCHITECTURAL
#  channel at lanes 0-2 and on the 8042 stub counter at lane 3 (the only lane-3 port with an access side effect on this
#  machine); the stub also carries lanes 0-2.  *** `as a strengthening, never as the sole carrier' IS MEASURED FALSE
#  HERE TOO -- the SECOND site of the same claim, corrected with it per the grep-for-siblings rule; see the header's
#  contract block for the three measurements and the latch-cannot-count reason. ***  Heads are EQUIVALENT (below).
#
#  4l `rdq': LANE-0 READS, direct widths 1/2/4/8.  Two self-test replies queued, one lca load of SP(0x60), then the flat
#  status: DIB must still be 1 (a duplicated read pops both).  Each phase drains the survivor with a flat data read.  The
#  control shows the channel counts pops: after two replies and ONE flat pop DIB is 1, after a second pop it is 0.
def selftest(): return [LDQ(T1, 0, T0), LDQ(T2, 8, T0), STB(T2, 0, T1)]             # queue one 0x55 (slot 0 = cmd port)
def kstatus(reg): return [LDQ(T1, 0, T0)] + sent(reg) + [LDBU(reg, 0, T1)]           # flat status (DIB = bit 0)
def kpop(): return [LDQ(T1, 16, T0), LDBU(T5, 0, T1)]                                # flat data read (slot 2) pops one
C4l = [k0(FLAT(KBC_CMD)), KBC_SELFTEST, k0(FLAT(KBC_DAT)), k0(SP(KBC_DAT))]
P4l = [(selftest() + selftest() + kpop() + kstatus(T4), "t4"),                        # control: one of two left
       (kpop() + kstatus(T4), "t4")]                                                  # control: none left
for w, n, ld, _ in (("b", 1, LDBU, STB), ("w", 2, LDWU, STW), ("l", 4, LDL, STL), ("q", 8, LDQ, STQ)):
    P4l.append((selftest() + selftest() + [LDQ(T1, 24, T0)] + sent(T3) + [ld(T3, 0, T1)] + kstatus(T4) + kpop(), "t4"))
a4l, g4l = dsession("rdq", C4l, P4l, ["ctl1", "ctl0", "r1", "r2", "r4", "r8"])
dibv = lambda v: None if v is None else v & 0x01
row("rdq alive", "alive" if a4l else "dead", "alive", "CONTROL")
row("rdq ctl1", hx(dibv(g4l["ctl1"])), hx(0x01), "CONTROL: two replies queued, one flat pop -> one left (DIB 1)")
row("rdq ctl0", hx(dibv(g4l["ctl0"])), hx(0x00), "CONTROL: a second flat pop empties it (DIB 0): the channel counts pops")
for n in (1, 2, 4, 8):
    row("rdq r%d" % n, hx(dibv(g4l["r%d" % n])), hx(0x01),
        "(%d,0) LOAD SP(0x60) pops ONE of two replies (RD2: the read forward duplicated)" % n)
print("")

#  4m `cmos': LANE 1, both arms, direct widths 1/2/4/8, on CMOS register C through the pccmos index/data pair (select
#  0x0c at 0x70; 0x71 forwards to dev_mc146818.c, whose REG C read returns the register and then ZEROES it, and whose
#  store sets it).  The stored value is 0x0f and every compare masks 0x0f: the mc146818 access path ORs UF/AF/PF/IRQF
#  (0xf0) into REG C from the HOST CLOCK, so only the low nibble is deterministic.  Store rows: an lca store of 0x0f
#  must still read back 0x0f (a phantom read after the store clears it).  Load rows: after a flat store of 0x0f the lca
#  load must deliver 0x0f at lane position (1 % len) (a duplicated forward delivers the second, cleared, read).
CMOS_VAL = 0x0f0f0f0f0f0f0f0f
def csel(): return [LDQ(T1, 0, T0), LDQ(T2, 8, T0), STB(T2, 0, T1)]                   # select REG C (flat 0x70)
def cset(): return csel() + [LDQ(T1, 24, T0), LDQ(T3, 32, T0), STB(T3, 0, T1)]        # flat store 0x0f -> REG C
def cget(reg): return csel() + [LDQ(T1, 24, T0)] + sent(reg) + [LDBU(reg, 0, T1)]     # flat read REG C (clears it)
C4m = [k0(FLAT(0x70)), 0x0c, k0(SP(0x71)), k0(FLAT(0x71)), CMOS_VAL]
P4m = [(cset() + cget(T4), "t4"), (cget(T4), "t4")]                                   # controls: set, then cleared
for w, n, ld, st in (("b", 1, LDBU, STB), ("w", 2, LDWU, STW), ("l", 4, LDL, STL), ("q", 8, LDQ, STQ)):
    P4m.append((csel() + [LDQ(T1, 16, T0), LDQ(T3, 32, T0), st(T3, 0, T1)] + cget(T4), "t4"))       # lca STORE
for w, n, ld, st in (("b", 1, LDBU, STB), ("w", 2, LDWU, STW), ("l", 4, LDL, STL), ("q", 8, LDQ, STQ)):
    P4m.append((cset() + csel() + [LDQ(T1, 16, T0)] + sent(T4) + [ld(T4, 0, T1)], "t4"))           # lca LOAD
a4m, g4m = dsession("cmos", C4m, P4m, ["set", "clr", "w1", "w2", "w4", "w8", "r1", "r2", "r4", "r8"])
nib = lambda v, sh=0: None if v is None else (v >> sh) & 0x0f
#  *** VALUE / POSITION rows, NOT count rows.  CMOS REG C is a ONE-SHOT LATCH (`dev_mc146818.c:565' -- the read
#  does `d->reg[MC_REGC*4] = 0x00;', so a SECOND read finds it already zero; the write path is a plain
#  assignment, so writing the same byte twice is idempotent).  The forward COUNT at lane 1 is carried by
#  `pit1' (repair 6).  These rows stay because they pin the POSITION rule, which is the defect #530 fixed. ***
row("cmos alive", "alive" if a4m else "dead", "alive", "CONTROL")
row("cmos set", hx(nib(g4m["set"])), hx(0x0f), "CONTROL: a flat store of 0x0f to REG C reads back 0x0f")
row("cmos clr", hx(nib(g4m["clr"])), hx(0x00), "CONTROL: that read cleared it -- the channel sees ONE extra read")
for n in (1, 2, 4, 8):
    row("cmos w%d" % n, hx(nib(g4m["w%d" % n])), hx(0x0f),
        "(%d,1) STORE 0x0f.. -> SP(0x71): REG C keeps it (x_wfallthru: a phantom read clears it)" % n)
for n in (1, 2, 4, 8):
    row("cmos r%d" % n, hx(nib(g4m["r%d" % n], 8 * (1 % n))), hx(0x0f),
        "(%d,1) LOAD SP(0x71) delivers REG C = 0x0f at position %d (RD2: the second read is cleared)" % (n, 1 % n))
print("")

#  4n `iirr2': LANE-2 READS at widths 2/4/8 -- a NEW session, `iirr' (the (1,2) read) is left as it was.  Re-arm TXRDY
#  before each load; the lane-2 byte of the loaded value must be IIR & 3 = TXRDY (a duplicated read clears it first).
C4n = [k0(FLAT(UART_IER)), k0(SP(UART_IIR))]
P4n = [(iir_arm() + [LDQ(T1, 8, T0)] + sent(T4) + [ld(T4, 0, T1)], "t4") for ld in (LDWU, LDL, LDQ)]
a4n, g4n = dsession("iirr2", C4n, P4n, ["r2", "r4", "r8"])
row("iirr2 alive", "alive" if a4n else "dead", "alive", "CONTROL")
for n, sh in ((2, 0), (4, 16), (8, 16)):
    v = g4n["r%d" % n]
    row("iir lca rd%d" % n, hx(None if v is None else (v >> sh) & 0x03), hx(0x02),
        "(%d,2) LOAD SP(0x3fa) after arming: TXRDY at position %d, read ONCE (R2w: RD2 gated on len)" % (n, 2 % n))
print("")

#  4o `stub0'-`stub3': the 8042 PC-style STUB.  dev_pckbc.c answers every port of 0x61-0x6f except 0x64 from a
#  FUNCTION-STATIC counter (`static int x; x++;') bumped on EVERY access, read or write, with bit 0x10 its parity -- a
#  "TODO (port 0x61)", an EMULATOR-MODEL oracle, not an architectural register (like the 0xffffffff absent-device
#  signature).  Flat read, ONE lca access, flat read: the two flat answers agree in bit 0x10; an extra access flips it.
#  The flats are chained, so each row brackets exactly one lca access.  Stores carry 0x5a at every byte position, so
#  the data-keyed fallthrough (dataA: the fallthrough only on a NONZERO byte) is inside the rows.  Lane 3 (0x63) is the
#  SOLE carrier of its cells; lanes 0-2 (0x68, 0x61, 0x62) are a strengthening beside 4k/4l/4m/4h-4j/4n.
#  *** LIVENESS, and it is MANDATORY: if port 0x61 is ever implemented every stub row goes green whatever the access
#  count.  Each session opens with two flat reads and NO lca access between them, which MUST flip bit 0x10. ***
STUB_DATA = 0x5a5a5a5a5a5a5a5a
for lane, port in ((3, 0x63), (0, 0x68), (1, 0x61), (2, 0x62)):
    C = [k0(FLAT(port)), k0(SP(port)), STUB_DATA]
    def sflat(): return [LDQ(T1, 0, T0)] + sent(T2) + [LDBU(T2, 0, T1)]
    P = [(sflat(), "t2"), (sflat(), "t2")]
    names = ["f0", "f1"]
    for w, n, ld, st in (("b", 1, LDBU, STB), ("w", 2, LDWU, STW), ("l", 4, LDL, STL), ("q", 8, LDQ, STQ)):
        P.append(([LDQ(T1, 8, T0)] + sent(T3) + [ld(T3, 0, T1)] + sflat(), "t2"))
        names.append("r%d" % n)
        P.append(([LDQ(T1, 8, T0), LDQ(T3, 16, T0), st(T3, 0, T1)] + sflat(), "t2"))
        names.append("w%d" % n)
    #  #530 repair 6: ONE ZERO STORE, lane 3 only -- it closes the lane-3 x ZERO-DATA joint the contract used to
    #  claim was covered by a marginal.  `cdxZL3' is `if ((relative_addr & 3) == 3 && data[ofs] == 0)' plus an extra
    #  read forward: it tests the LANE and the DATA and NOT `len', so one row at any width reds it, and every store
    #  row above carries 0x5a -- nonzero -- so none of them could.  Measured 185/185 (an ESCAPE) before this row.
    if lane == 3:
        P.append(([LDQ(T1, 8, T0), STB(ZERO, 0, T1)] + sflat(), "t2"))
        names.append("z1")
    a, g = dsession("stub%d" % lane, C, P, names)
    flip = lambda x, y: None if x is None or y is None else (x ^ y) & 0x10
    row("stub%d alive" % lane, "alive" if a else "dead", "alive", "CONTROL")
    row("stub%d live" % lane, hx(flip(g["f0"], g["f1"])), hx(0x10),
        "LIVENESS: two flat reads of 0x%x flip bit 4 -- the stub's access counter is present" % port)
    prev = "f1"
    for nm in names[2:]:
        n = int(nm[1:])
        if nm[0] == "z":
            row("stub%d %s" % (lane, nm), hx(flip(g[prev], g[nm])), hx(0x00),
                "(1,%d) STORE ZERO at SP(0x%x): ONE access reaches the port (cdxZL3 -- the fallthrough gated on "
                "lane 3 AND a zero byte; every other store row here carries 0x5a and cannot see it)" % (lane, port))
        else:
            row("stub%d %s" % (lane, nm), hx(flip(g[prev], g[nm])), hx(0x00),
                "(%d,%d) %s SP(0x%x): ONE access reaches the port (%s)"
                % (n, lane, "LOAD" if nm[0] == "r" else "STORE 0x5a..", port,
                   "RD2" if nm[0] == "r" else "x_wfallthru, nonzero byte"))
        prev = nm
    print("")

#  4p `tailq_w' / 4q `tailq_r': the split-TAIL cells, widths 3/5/6/7 at LANE 0 (a tail always starts at a port 0 mod
#  0x20; here the 8042 data port 0x60).  `tailw' (4f) stores into an EMPTY queue, so a phantom read there changes nothing
#  -- WT6/WTsplit scored 111/111.  WRITE tails: one reply queued, 0xd1 (the next data write goes to output_byte), the
#  split STQ's tail forwards 0, and the flat status DIB must still be 1.  READ tails: two replies queued, the split LDQ's
#  tail pops ONE, DIB must still be 1.  Each phase drains the survivor with a flat data read.
C4p = [k0(FLAT(KBC_CMD)), KBC_SELFTEST, k0(FLAT(KBC_DAT)), KBC_WROUTPORT] + [k0(SP(0x5f) + o) for _, o in TAILHEADS]
def wrout(): return [LDQ(T1, 0, T0), LDQ(T2, 24, T0), STB(T2, 0, T1)]                # 0xd1 -> the command port
P4p = [(selftest() + kstatus(T4) + kpop(), "t4")]                                     # control: one reply queued
for i in range(len(TAILHEADS)):
    P4p.append((selftest() + wrout() + [LDQ(T1, 32 + 8 * i, T0), STQ(ZERO, 0, T1)] + kstatus(T4) + kpop(), "t4"))
a4p, g4p = dsession("tailq_w", C4p, P4p, ["ctl"] + ["t%d" % (8 - h) for h, _ in TAILHEADS])
row("tailq w alive", "alive" if a4p else "dead", "alive", "CONTROL")
row("tailq w ctl", hx(dibv(g4p["ctl"])), hx(0x01), "CONTROL: a queued reply sets DIB")
for h, _ in TAILHEADS:
    row("tailq w t%d" % (8 - h), hx(dibv(g4p["t%d" % (8 - h)])), hx(0x01),
        "(%d,0) STQ TAIL at 0x60: the queued reply survives the store (x_wfallthru at a tail width)" % (8 - h))
P4q = [(selftest() + selftest() + kpop() + kstatus(T4) + kpop(), "t4")]               # control: one of two left
for i in range(len(TAILHEADS)):
    P4q.append((selftest() + selftest() + [LDQ(T1, 32 + 8 * i, T0)] + sent(T3) + [LDQ(T3, 0, T1)]
                + kstatus(T4) + kpop(), "t4"))
a4q, g4q = dsession("tailq_r", C4p, P4q, ["ctl"] + ["t%d" % (8 - h) for h, _ in TAILHEADS])
row("tailq r alive", "alive" if a4q else "dead", "alive", "CONTROL")
row("tailq r ctl", hx(dibv(g4q["ctl"])), hx(0x01), "CONTROL: two replies queued, one flat pop -> one left")
for h, _ in TAILHEADS:
    row("tailq r t%d" % (8 - h), hx(dibv(g4q["t%d" % (8 - h)])), hx(0x01),
        "(%d,0) LDQ TAIL at 0x60 pops ONE of two replies (RD2 at a tail width pops both)" % (8 - h))
print("")

#  ---- SESSIONS 5-10: the six cells that ENDED THE HOST on HEAD, one process each -------------
#  Each cell gets its own process and its own `alive' row, so a reinstated exit is attributed to its
#  cell -- with ONE overlap (codex, pass 2): w13's round trip ALSO reads (1,3), so an exit keyed on the
#  (1,3) read alone ends both r13 and w13.  And a value row, because `alive' greens equally for a
#  decline (the C and A mutants), so the value is what separates the fix from them.
EXITS = [
  #  tag   consts                                              phases                                      names            rows (name, key, want-fn, note)
  ("r12", [k0(FLAT(UART_MSR)), k0(SP(UART_MSR))],
          [([LDQ(T1, 0, T0)] + sent(T2) + [LDBU(T2, 0, T1)], "t2"),
           ([LDQ(T1, 8, T0)] + sent(T3) + [LDBU(T3, 0, T1)], "t3")],
          ["msr", "r12"],
          [("r12 byte", "r12", lambda g: g["msr"],
            "(1,2) LDBU MSR == flat MSR: was host death; kills C/A (sentinel)")]),
  ("r13", [k0(FLAT(UART_SCR)), SCR_VALUE, k0(SP(UART_SCR))],
          [([LDQ(T1, 0, T0), LDQ(T2, 8, T0), STB(T2, 0, T1),
             LDQ(T1, 16, T0)] + sent(T3) + [LDBU(T3, 0, T1)], "t3")],
          ["r13"],
          [("r13 byte", "r13", lambda g: SCR_VALUE,
            "(1,3) LDBU SCR after a flat store of 0x5a: was host death; kills C/A")]),
  ("r23", [k0(FLAT(UART_SCR)), SCR_VALUE, k0(SP(UART_SCR))],
          [([LDQ(T1, 0, T0), LDQ(T2, 8, T0), STB(T2, 0, T1),
             LDQ(T1, 16, T0)] + sent(T3) + [LDWU(T3, 0, T1)], "t3")],
          ["r23"],
          [("r23 lane", "r23", lambda g: SCR_VALUE << 8,
            "(2,3) LDWU SCR -> position 1, paired with `r21 anchor': kills the clamp (position 0)")]),
  ("w12", [k0(SP(UART_MSR)), k0(FLAT(UART_MSR))],
          [(load_const(T3, 0x40) + [LDQ(T1, 0, T0), STB(T3, 0, T1),
            LDQ(T1, 8, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T1)], "t4")],
          ["w12"],
          [("w12 lands", "w12", lambda g: 0x40 | MSR_FIXED,
            "(1,2) STB 0x40 -> MSR, flat readback: was host death; kills C/A (store dropped)")]),
  ("w13", [k0(SP(UART_SCR)), k0(FLAT(UART_SCR))],
          [(load_const(T3, 0x3c) + [LDQ(T1, 0, T0), STB(T3, 0, T1),
            LDQ(T1, 8, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T1)], "t4"),
           ([LDQ(T1, 0, T0)] + sent(T5) + [LDBU(T5, 0, T1)], "t5")],
          ["w13", "w13rt"],
          [("w13 lands", "w13", lambda g: 0x3c,
            "(1,3) STB 0x3c -> SCR, flat readback: was host death; kills C/A and the unfolded write"),
           ("w13 roundtrip", "w13rt", lambda g: 0x3c,
            "SELF-COHERENCE: stb then ldbu at ONE sparse address returns what was stored")]),
  ("w23", [k0(SP(UART_SCR)), k0(FLAT(UART_SCR))],
          [(load_const(T3, 0x7e00) + [LDQ(T1, 0, T0), STW(T3, 0, T1),
            LDQ(T1, 8, T0)] + sent(T4) + [LDBU(T4, 0, T1)], "t4")],
          ["w23"],
          [("w23 lane", "w23", lambda g: 0x7e,
            "(2,3) STW 0x7e00 -> SCR forwards position 1 (as `w21 lane'): kills the clamp")]),
]
for tag, consts, phases, names, rws in EXITS:
    ax, gx = dsession(tag, consts, phases, names)
    row("%s alive" % tag, "alive" if ax else "dead", "alive",
        "was a host death on HEAD (`ofs > len'); kills the reinstated exit")
    for nm, key, wantf, note in rws:
        w = wantf(gx)
        row(nm, hx(gx[key]), hx(w), note)
    print("")

#  ================================================================================================================
#  #530 REPAIR 6 -- THE 8253 SESSIONS.  The forward-COUNT class's channel at lanes 0/1/2 and the split tails.
#
#  *** WHY THE CHANNEL CHANGED, AND IT IS THE FINDING OF CLOSING PASS 5.  Repair 5 put CMOS REG C at lane 1 and
#  ns16550 IIR at lane 2.  `dev_mc146818.c:565' -- the read does `d->reg[MC_REGC*4] = 0x00;', so a second read finds
#  it already zero; the write path is a plain assignment.  `dev_ns16550.c:211-213' -- the read clears IIR_TXRDY once;
#  the write path is `d->fcr = idata;'.  BOTH ARE ONE-SHOT LATCHES, AND A LATCH SEPARATES *ZERO* ACCESSES FROM
#  *AT LEAST ONE*.  IT CAN NEVER SEPARATE *ONE* FROM *TWO*, which is exactly and only what a forward-COUNT class
#  asks.  Measured: WWall 169/185 is sixteen stub rows and NOTHING else; RWall 167/185 is 16 stub + 2 tailr;
#  RD2post 161/185 has no architectural carrier at lanes 1-2.  The emulator-TODO stub was carrying those cells alone
#  because THE STUB IS AN ALTERNATOR AND ITS NEIGHBOURS WERE LATCHES. ***
#
#      THE RULE, checkable by reading: A FORWARD-COUNT ROW NEEDS A COUNTER OR AN ALTERNATOR, NEVER A LATCH.
#
#  *** THE ROW TAXONOMY THAT FOLLOWS FROM IT, because conflating the two kinds is what produced three passes of
#  escapes.  A row here is one of TWO things and the contract must say which: ***
#      COUNT rows -- they answer "HOW MANY forwards reached the port?"  Their channel is a COUNTER or an ALTERNATOR:
#        the 8253 flip-flops (`pit0/1/2', `pitT'), the 8042 reply queue used as `kbcmd' (did the command produce
#        EXACTLY ONE reply?), and the pckbc stub's access parity (`stub0'-`stub3').
#      VALUE / POSITION rows -- they answer "WHICH BYTE landed WHERE?"  Their channel is a one-shot LATCH or a stored
#        value: `cmos' (REG C: the read clears it), `iir'/`iirw'/`iirr' (the read clears TXRDY once), the
#        store/read-back rows, `ofs*', `h*', `tailr'.  THESE ARE GOOD ROWS AND THEY STAY -- they carry the POSITION
#        rule, which is what #530 actually fixed.  What they CANNOT do is separate ONE forward from TWO, so no
#        forward-count cell may rest on them, and before repair 6 three cells did.
#
#  THE MECHANISM, read from source and not from the brief (`src/devices/dev_8253.c'):
#    * ports 0x40/0x41/0x42 are `relative_addr' 0/1/2 = LANES 0/1/2; port 0x43 is the mode register = lane 3.
#    * the flip-flop advance sits INSIDE the counter case and fires on EVERY counter access, read and write, while
#      (mode_byte & 0x30) == I8253_TIMER_16BIT.  `wr_msb' toggles on writes, `rd_msb' on reads -- SEPARATE PER
#      DIRECTION, which is why a read-parity row is blind to an added WRITE and the PW shape exists.
#    * a control word REWINDS both flip-flops for that counter, so every phase is self-starting.  EVERY PHASE MUST
#      OPEN WITH ONE, or phases leak parity into each other.
#
#  *** LANE 3 IS UNREACHABLE ON THIS CHANNEL, AND THAT IS MEASURED, NOT REASONED: a read of the mode port returns
#  `mode_byte' with no side effect and advances no flip-flop, and a repeated identical control word is idempotent.
#  `cdxZL3', whose entire edit is at lane 3, scores 150/150 against these rows.  Lane 3 therefore KEEPS `stub3' and
#  its mandatory liveness row -- which RB1 already licenses -- and this contract says so rather than implying the
#  8253 covers everything. ***
#
#  COST, measured by the prototype rather than estimated: a 16-bit MSB write to counters 1 and 2 emits an UNLATCHED
#  `fatal()' (`dev_8253.c:198' `TODO: DMA refresh?' and `:200' `TODO: 8253 tone generation?'), 18 of each per session.
#  It moves no row -- the probe parses `print' output -- and it is filed separately as `pit8253flood'.
#  An MSB write to counter 0 instead calls `timer_add'/`timer_update_frequency' with hz = 1193182/counter, so the
#  programmed counter is kept LARGE (~250 Hz); a small one would install a high-frequency host timer.
#  ================================================================================================================

def b(v, sh=0):
    return None if v is None else (v >> sh) & 0xff


PITCW  = 0x43
WIDTHS = (("b", 1, LDBU, STB), ("w", 2, LDWU, STW), ("l", 4, LDL, STL), ("q", 8, LDQ, STQ))

def _cw():             return [LDQ(T1, 0, T0), LDQ(T2, 8, T0), STB(T2, 0, T1)]
def _fl_st(s, a=2):    return [LDQ(T1, 8 * a, T0), LDQ(T2, 8 * s, T0), STB(T2, 0, T1)]
def _fl_st0(a=2):      return [LDQ(T1, 8 * a, T0), STB(ZERO, 0, T1)]
def _fl_rd(r, a=2):    return [LDQ(T1, 8 * a, T0)] + sent(r) + [LDBU(r, 0, T1)]

#  ---------------------------------------------------------------- counters 0/1/2 = lanes 0/1/2
for _N in (0, 1, 2):
    _port = 0x40 + _N
    _CWV  = 0x34 | (_N << 6)          # counter N, RW = 11 (LSB then MSB), mode 2, binary
    _C = [k0(FLAT(PITCW)), _CWV, k0(FLAT(_port)), k0(SP(_port)), 0x34, 0x12,
          0x5a5a5a5a5a5a5a5a, 0x77]

    def _lca_st(st, X):
        return ([LDQ(T1, 24, T0), LDQ(T2, 48, T0), st(T2, 0, T1)] if X
                else [LDQ(T1, 24, T0), st(ZERO, 0, T1)])

    def _lca_ld(ld, r):
        return [LDQ(T1, 24, T0)] + sent(r) + [ld(r, 0, T1)]

    _P = [(_cw() + _fl_st(4) + _fl_st(5) + _fl_rd(T3) + _fl_rd(T4), ["t3", "t4"])]
    for _X in (0x5a, 0x00):
        for _w, _n, _ld, _st in WIDTHS:
            _P.append((_cw() + _lca_st(_st, _X) + _fl_st(5) + _fl_rd(T3) + _fl_rd(T4), ["t3", "t4"]))
    for _L in (0x5a, 0x00):
        for _w, _n, _ld, _st in WIDTHS:
            _P.append((_cw() + (_fl_st(6) if _L else _fl_st0()) + _fl_st(5)
                       + _lca_ld(_ld, T3) + _fl_rd(T4), ["t3", "t4"]))
    _P.append((_cw() + _fl_st(6) + _fl_st(5) + _lca_ld(LDQ, T3) + _fl_st(7)
               + _fl_rd(T4) + _fl_rd(T5), ["t3", "t4", "t5"]))

    #  READINESS IS COUNT-AWARE: 18 prints of T3 and T4, one of T5.  A session that times out halfway would
    #  otherwise report READY on the strength of its FIRST print and score every row against missing data.
    _t, _a, _r = run("pit%d" % _N, _C, _P, [("t3", 18), ("t4", 18), ("t5", 1)])
    if _r:
        DRETRIED.append("pit%d" % _N)
    evidence(_t, "pit%d" % _N)
    print("  RAW[pit%02x]: alive=%s live=%s/%s retried=%s"
          % (_port, _a, hx(nth(_t, "t3", 0)), hx(nth(_t, "t4", 0)), _r))

    row("pit%d alive" % _N, "alive" if _a else "dead", "alive",
        "CONTROL: nothing in this session may end the host")
    row("pit%d live1" % _N, hx(nth(_t, "t3", 0)), hx(0x34),
        "LIVENESS+DEVSIG: after a CW, flat LSB 0x34 / MSB 0x12 read back LSB FIRST")
    row("pit%d live2" % _N, hx(nth(_t, "t4", 0)), hx(0x12),
        "LIVENESS+DEVSIG: ...then the MSB.  Two DIFFERENT bytes in a FIXED order from ONE address: RAM repeats, the "
        "ns16550 scratch returns what was written -- only a 16-bit flip-flop alternates")

    _k = 1
    for _X in (0x5a, 0x00):
        for _w, _n, _ld, _st in WIDTHS:
            row("pit%d ps%02x w%d" % (_N, _X, _n), hx(b(nth(_t, "t3", _k))), hx(_X),
                "(%d,%d) STORE 0x%02x: ONE lca write leaves it in the LSB (an EXTRA access -> 0x12)" % (_n, _N, _X))
            row("pit%d ps%02x w%dB" % (_N, _X, _n), hx(b(nth(_t, "t4", _k))), hx(0x12),
                "(%d,%d) ...and the MSB holds the flat 0x12 (an EXTRA access -> 0x%02x)" % (_n, _N, _X))
            _k += 1
    for _L in (0x5a, 0x00):
        for _w, _n, _ld, _st in WIDTHS:
            row("pit%d pl%02x r%d" % (_N, _L, _n), hx(b(nth(_t, "t3", _k), 8 * (_N % _n))), hx(_L),
                "(%d,%d) LOAD delivers the counter LSB 0x%02x at position %d" % (_n, _N, _L, _N % _n))
            row("pit%d pl%02x r%dB" % (_N, _L, _n), hx(b(nth(_t, "t4", _k))), hx(0x12),
                "(%d,%d) ...and the NEXT flat read gets the MSB (a DUPLICATED read -> 0x%02x)" % (_n, _N, _L))
            _k += 1
    row("pit%d pw r1" % _N, hx(b(nth(_t, "t4", _k))), hx(0x12),
        "(8,%d) LOAD then a flat store 0x77: read parity unmoved -> MSB 0x12" % _N)
    row("pit%d pw r2" % _N, hx(b(nth(_t, "t5", 0))), hx(0x77),
        "(8,%d) ...and 0x77 went to the LSB.  An added WRITE on the read arm sends it to the MSB -- this is the ONLY "
        "shape that sees one, because wr_msb and rd_msb are SEPARATE" % _N)
    print("")

#  ---------------------------------------------------------------- the split TAILS at port 0x40 (counter 0, lane 0)
#  SP(0x3f) + K straddles the 1 KB split at SP(0x40): K = 0x1f/0x1e/0x1d/0x1b gives head 1/2/3/5 at the UNMAPPED port
#  0x3f and TAIL 7/6/5/3 at port 0x40.  The tail's buffer position 0 is register byte (8 - tail) = the head length.
_C = [k0(FLAT(PITCW)), 0x34, k0(FLAT(0x40)), k0(SP(0x3f) + 0x1f), k0(SP(0x3f) + 0x1e),
      k0(SP(0x3f) + 0x1d), k0(SP(0x3f) + 0x1b), 0x34, 0x12, 0x77, 0x5a5a5a5a5a5a5a5a]
TAILS = ((7, 3, 8), (6, 4, 16), (5, 5, 24), (3, 6, 40))   # (tail len, address slot, delivered-byte shift)

_P = [(_cw() + _fl_st(7) + _fl_st(8) + _fl_rd(T3) + _fl_rd(T4), ["t3", "t4"])]
for _t_, _aslot, _sh in TAILS:
    for _X in (0x5a, 0x00):
        _st = ([LDQ(T1, 8 * _aslot, T0), LDQ(T2, 80, T0), STQ(T2, 0, T1)] if _X
               else [LDQ(T1, 8 * _aslot, T0), STQ(ZERO, 0, T1)])
        _P.append((_cw() + _st + _fl_st(8) + _fl_rd(T3) + _fl_rd(T4), ["t3", "t4"]))
for _t_, _aslot, _sh in TAILS:
    for _L in (0x5a, 0x00):
        _P.append((_cw() + (_fl_st(10) if _L else _fl_st0()) + _fl_st(8)
                   + [LDQ(T1, 8 * _aslot, T0)] + sent(T3) + [LDQ(T3, 0, T1)] + _fl_rd(T4), ["t3", "t4"]))
for _t_, _aslot, _sh in ((6, 4, 16), (3, 6, 40)):
    _P.append((_cw() + _fl_st(10) + _fl_st(8) + [LDQ(T1, 8 * _aslot, T0)] + sent(T3) + [LDQ(T3, 0, T1)]
               + _fl_st(9) + _fl_rd(T4) + _fl_rd(T5), ["t3", "t4", "t5"]))

_t, _a, _r = run("pitT", _C, _P, [("t3", 19), ("t4", 19), ("t5", 2)])
if _r:
    DRETRIED.append("pitT")
evidence(_t, "pitT")
print("  RAW[tails]: alive=%s live=%s/%s retried=%s" % (_a, hx(nth(_t, "t3", 0)), hx(nth(_t, "t4", 0)), _r))

row("pitT alive", "alive" if _a else "dead", "alive", "CONTROL: nothing in this session may end the host")
row("pitT live1", hx(nth(_t, "t3", 0)), hx(0x34), "LIVENESS+DEVSIG: flat LSB then MSB at port 0x40")
row("pitT live2", hx(nth(_t, "t4", 0)), hx(0x12), "LIVENESS+DEVSIG: ...the second read gets the OTHER half")

_k = 1
for _t_, _aslot, _sh in TAILS:
    for _X in (0x5a, 0x00):
        row("pitT ps%02x t%d" % (_X, _t_), hx(b(nth(_t, "t3", _k))), hx(_X),
            "(%d,0) STQ TAIL at 0x40 stores 0x%02x: ONE write -> LSB (an EXTRA access -> 0x12)" % (_t_, _X))
        row("pitT ps%02x t%dB" % (_X, _t_), hx(b(nth(_t, "t4", _k))), hx(0x12),
            "(%d,0) ...the MSB holds the flat 0x12 (an EXTRA access -> 0x%02x)" % (_t_, _X))
        _k += 1
for _t_, _aslot, _sh in TAILS:
    for _L in (0x5a, 0x00):
        row("pitT pl%02x t%d" % (_L, _t_), hx(b(nth(_t, "t3", _k), _sh)), hx(_L),
            "(%d,0) LDQ TAIL delivers the LSB 0x%02x at register byte %d" % (_t_, _L, _sh // 8))
        row("pitT pl%02x t%dB" % (_L, _t_), hx(b(nth(_t, "t4", _k))), hx(0x12),
            "(%d,0) ...and the next flat read gets the MSB (a DUPLICATED read -> 0x%02x)" % (_t_, _L))
        _k += 1

    row("pitT pw t%d" % _t_, hx(b(nth(_t, "t4", _k + _i))), hx(0x12),
        "(%d,0) LDQ TAIL then a flat store 0x77: read parity unmoved -> MSB 0x12" % _t_)
    row("pitT pw t%dB" % _t_, hx(b(nth(_t, "t5", _i))), hx(0x77),
        "(%d,0) ...and 0x77 went to the LSB (an added WRITE on the read arm sends it to the MSB)" % _t_)
print("")

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- summary
#  Computed adjacent to the print on purpose: #516 shipped a probe whose numerator was counted
#  over an older row set while the denominator was evaluated at print time, and every row said
#  `ok' beside a summary that did not.
ok = sum(1 for _, o in rows if o)
retr = ",".join([t for t, r in (("base", r1), ("ofs2", r2), ("ofs3", r3)) if r] + DRETRIED)
print("")
print("LCAISALANE_RETRIED=%s" % (retr if retr else "none"))
print("LCAISALANE_RESULT=%d/%d" % (ok, len(rows)))
print("LCAISALANE_WITNESS=%s" % ("PASS" if ok == len(rows) else "FAIL"))
print("LCAISALANE_DONE")
sys.exit(0 if ok == len(rows) else 1)

28-#
29-#  *** THE LANE IS NOT AN INDEPENDENT FIELD.  IT IS THE LOW TWO BITS OF THE ISA PORT NUMBER. ***
30-#  The shift comes FIRST, so `ofs' is bits 6:5 of the incoming relative address -- which are bits
31:#  1:0 of the derived port -- and the forwarded access goes to `LCA_ISA_BASE + (r >> 5)', i.e. to
32-#  that same port INCLUDING those two bits.  So there is no way to address ONE port through two
33-#  different lanes: the lane is a FUNCTION of the port.  The defect therefore partitions the whole
34-#  64 K ISA port space by `port & 3', and for a BYTE (len == 1) access it reads:
35-#
36-#      port & 3 == 0   ->  ofs 0.  data[0] = byte.                        CORRECT
37-#      port & 3 == 1   ->  ofs 1.  1 > 1 is FALSE, so ACCEPTED -- and the packing loop runs
38-#                          `for (i = 0; i < 1; i++) data[i] = (i == 1)? byte : 0', which is
39-#                          never true, so THE DEVICE BYTE IS DISCARDED AND THE GUEST IS HANDED
40-#                          ZERO with a success return.  A false success, not a decline.
41-#      port & 3 == 2   ->  ofs 2.  2 > 1  ->  fatal() + exit(1).          HOST DEATH

222-#
223-#  *** WHAT THE WITNESS (sessions 1-3) IS AND IS NOT.  IT IS A WITNESS, NOT A DETECTOR. ***
224-#
225-#  It establishes, on unmodified `alphabook1' with no disk image and no OS: that the sparse window
226-#  IS live and does reach ISA devices (`live', 0x55 from the 8042 through the same handler); that a
227-#  BYTE read of a device register whose port is 1 mod 4 completes successfully and hands the guest
228:#  zero while the device's own byte is demonstrably present (`ofs1 devsig' + `ofs1 wide'); and that
229-#  a byte read of a register whose port is 2 or 3 mod 4 ends the host with the arm's own diagnostic.
230-#
231-#  It is NOT a detector of any repair.  `alive' greens equally for a decline, for a latched
232-#  announce, for a fault and for a full implementation, and `ofs1 zero' greens for any fix that
233-#  hands the byte back.  A fix round owes rows that discriminate those -- at minimum, per the codex
234-#  design reading (regress/artifacts/lcaisalane/codex_design_20260910.err): a REFUSED access must
235-#  leave the destination register holding its previous value (the `answer' channel this probe
236-#  already reads), the refusal must repeat without re-announcing, and a supported lane must still
237-#  deliver the correctly packed byte at every width.
238-#
--
240-#  SHAPE, and every element of it is answering a specific objection:
241-#
242-#    1.  ONE PROCESS PER OUTCOME, CONTROL FIRST.  A death ends the process, so the two killing
243-#        lanes get their own runs -- but inside each run the DEVICE SIGNATURE and the WIDE control
244-#        are taken BEFORE the killing access, so between the access that lives and the access that
245-#        dies only the WIDTH differs: same address, same window, same process, same instant.
246-#    2.  ZERO READBACK ALONE PROVES NOTHING, and the design seat said so in those words.  Unmapped
247-#        space, a missed device and a zero-filled failure buffer all read 0 (`memory_rw.c''s #95
248-#        pre-zero).  So `ofs1 zero' is never asserted on its own: `ofs1 devsig' reads the SAME
249-#        register through the flat ISA mapping and gets a nonzero value the device alone produces,
250:#        and `ofs1 wide' reads it through THIS handler at len 4 and finds the byte sitting in lane
251-#        1 -- so the byte reaches the packing loop and the loop is what drops it.
252-#    3.  THE DESTINATION REGISTER IS PRE-SEEDED WITH A SENTINEL before every load.  A load that
253-#        FAULTS leaves the register untouched (cpu_alpha_instr_loadstore.c returns before
254-#        writeback when memory_rw fails), so `ofs1 answer' distinguishes "the model answered zero"
255-#        from "the access failed and the guest kept its old value".  On HEAD it is the former: a
256-#        FALSE SUCCESS.  This is the discriminator the design seat asked for, taken pre-fix.
257-#    4.  ATTRIBUTION IS BY THE ARM'S OWN TEXT, NEVER BY EXIT STATUS.  `in lca_isa access function'
258-#        appears exactly once in src/.  A death for any other reason -- a bad encoding, a different
259-#        arm -- reds the attrib row instead of passing as a reproduction.
260-#    5.  EVERY ACCESS IS A GUEST INSTRUCTION.  `lca_isa' is registered DM_DEFAULT, so debugger
261-#        `dump' would skip the handler entirely and read the pre-zero.  Every `put d' below targets
262:#        SCRATCH, which is RAM.
263-#    6.  STAY ON `step'.  Nothing here needs a host timer: the 8042 reply and the UART register
264-#        file are synchronous with the guest access.
265-#
266-#  THE NEGATIVE CONTROL IS ISA PORT 0x300 (`ctl'), which is 0 mod 4 and has NO device behind it.
267-#  It survives and answers zero.  That is what attributes the two deaths to the LANE rather than to
268-#  the sparse window, to the byte width, to K0SEG, or to hitting empty ISA space -- and note the
269-#  two killing ports DO have a device behind them, so the pair brackets the question from both
270-#  sides.
271-#
272-#  Usage:  lca_isalane_probe.py <gxemul-binary> [label]

51:# ---- encoder, copied from lca_isalane_probe.py so the encodings are identical
52:ZERO, T0, T1, T2, T3, T4 = 31, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5

55:def mem(op, ra, rb, disp):
56:    assert -32768 <= disp <= 32767, "displacement %d out of range" % disp
57:    return (op << 26) | (ra << 21) | (rb << 16) | (disp & 0xffff)
58:def LDA (ra, disp, rb=ZERO): return mem(0x08, ra, rb, disp)
59:def LDAH(ra, disp, rb=ZERO): return mem(0x09, ra, rb, disp)
60:def LDBU(ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x0a, ra, rb, disp)
61:def STB (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x0e, ra, rb, disp)
62:def LDQ (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x29, ra, rb, disp)
63:CALL_PAL_PROM = (0x00 << 26) | 0x3fffffe      # == 0x03fffffe, the word machine_alpha.c:189 stores

65:def load_const(reg, val):                     # 32-bit only, exactly as the parent
66:    lo = val & 0xffff
67:    hi = (val >> 16) & 0xffff
68:    if lo >= 0x8000:
69:        hi = (hi + 1) & 0xffff
70:    ops = [LDAH(reg, hi if hi < 0x8000 else hi - 0x10000)]
71:    if lo:
72:        ops.append(LDA(reg, lo if lo < 0x8000 else lo - 0x10000, reg))
73:    return ops

89:        os.execv(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "alpha", "-e", MACH, "-M", "64",
90:                       "-o", BOOTSTR, "0x20000000:" + DUMMY])
91:        os._exit(127)
92:    buf = ""
93:    def rd(t):
94:        nonlocal buf
95:        end = time.time() + t
96:        while time.time() < end:
97:            r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], 0.1)
98:            if not r:
99:                continue
100:            try:
101:                d = os.read(fd, 65536)
102:                if not d:
103:                    return False
104:                buf += d.decode("latin-1", "replace")
105:            except OSError:
106:                return False
107:        return True
108:    alive = rd(settle)
109:    for i, c in enumerate(cmds):
110:        try:
111:            os.write(fd, (c + "\n").encode("latin-1"))
112:        except OSError:
113:            alive = False; break
114:        if not rd(spin if i == len(cmds) - 1 else 0.45):
115:            alive = False; break
116:    try:
117:        os.write(fd, b"quit\n"); rd(0.5)
118:    except OSError:
119:        pass
120:    try:
121:        os.close(fd)
122:    except OSError:
123:        pass
124:    return buf.replace("\r", ""), alive

126:def build(consts, phases):                    # copied verbatim in behaviour
127:    prog = load_const(T0, SCRATCH)
128:    lens = [len(prog)]
129:    for ops, _ in phases:
130:        prog += ops
131:        lens.append(len(ops))
132:    cmds  = ["put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (SCRATCH + 8 * i, v) for i, v in enumerate(consts)]
133:    cmds += ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (k0(CODE) + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(prog)]
134:    cmds += ["pc=0x%x" % k0(CODE), "step %d" % lens[0]]
135:    for (ops, pr), n in zip(phases, lens[1:]):
136:        cmds.append("step %d" % n)
137:        if pr is not None:
138:            cmds.append("print %s" % pr)
139:    return cmds

151: *  1024 bytes and 1 MB / 1024 = 1024.  The old "256" came from dividing 1 MB by a 4 KB page
152: *  -- a granule this split does not use, as the note above says: "The granule the SPLIT
153: *  needs is a different one."  (And per that note the real page here is 8 KB anyway.)
154: *
155: *  Two properties of the memblock split, both MEASURED:
156: *    - post-#430 it fires only on PHYSICAL accesses.  A TRANSLATED 4096-byte write across a
157: *      1 MB boundary is chunked at the 1024-byte SPLIT granule, so it costs 4 translations
158: *      and 4 host lookups and never reaches here; PHYSICAL costs 0 and 2.  A chunk of at
159: *      most one granule, granule-aligned, cannot cross 1 MB.
160: *    - its head can NEVER be a device, in either version: device dispatch `goto
161: *      do_return_ok`s first.  Measured with a device straddling the memblock boundary --
162: *      one device call, offset 0xffe, len 4, zero host lookups.
163: *
164: *  A LOOP, not recursion.  A recursive split costs a real frame per chunk when the compiler
165: *  does NOT eliminate the tail call -- measured at -O0, 1,834,896 bytes of stack for a 16 MB
166: *  access -- and a large enough access then dies on the stack rather than returning an
167: *  answer.
168: *
169: *  CORRECTION, and it is the reason this paragraph is worded carefully: the first draft said
170: *  "gcc does not turn the tail call into a loop at -O2".  MEASURED, IT DOES -- gcc 15.2.1
171: *  performs the sibling-call optimisation at -O2 and -O3, leaving a 96-byte span.  So the
172: *  loop is still the right implementation (it does not depend on an optimisation being
173: *  applied, and -O0 builds and other compilers are real), but the justification was wrong,
174: *  and the row that certified it was VACUOUS until the differential was given
175: *  -fno-optimize-sibling-calls.  No caller in the tree passes an access large enough for
176: *  this to bite today; it is insurance, and it costs about fifteen lines.
177: *
178: *  ONLY WHEN A TRANSLATION ACTUALLY HAPPENS.  Under PHYSICAL (or with no translate_v2p)
179: *  paddr == vaddr, there is no per-page mapping to redo, and splitting would buy nothing
180: *  while turning one large DMA into thousands of calls -- so those go straight through and
181: *  the memblock split keeps doing its job unchanged.
182: */
183:int MEMORY_RW(struct cpu *cpu, struct memory *mem, uint64_t vaddr,
184:	unsigned char *data, size_t len, int writeflag, int misc_flags)
185:{
186:	if (!(misc_flags & PHYSICAL) && cpu->translate_v2p != NULL) {
187:		size_t granule = (size_t) MRW_SPLIT_MASK + 1;
188:		size_t offset = (size_t) (vaddr & MRW_SPLIT_MASK);
189:
190:		/*  `len > granule - offset`, NOT `offset + len > granule`: the sum can
191:		    wrap for an absurd length (offset 1, len SIZE_MAX), which would skip
192:		    the loop and hand the whole thing to the worker.  The subtraction
193:		    cannot wrap, because offset < granule always.
194:
195:		    STATED EXACTLY, because the first version of this comment implied more
196:		    than it delivers: this form does not SERVICE such a length, it declines
197:		    to mis-copy it.  A length near SIZE_MAX now iterates about 2^54 times
198:		    instead of silently under-splitting -- measured, a fuzz watchdog fired
199:		    at two million chunks.  No caller passes anything remotely like it (the
200:		    largest in the tree are PHYSICAL and exempt), so the case is recorded
201:		    and filed rather than handled.  */
202:		while (len > granule - offset) {
203:			size_t head = granule - offset;
204:
205:			if (!MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE(cpu, mem, vaddr, data, head,
206:			    writeflag, misc_flags)) {
207:				/*  #244 PROMISES THE WHOLE READ BUFFER, AND SPLITTING BROKE
208:				    THAT: the worker zeroes only the chunk IT was given, so a
209:				    failure before the last chunk used to leave the caller's
210:				    remaining bytes untouched -- uninitialised host stack, for
211:				    a caller that ignores the return value.  Unsplit, one call
212:				    covered the whole length.  Zero the rest here so the
213:				    guarantee is the same as it was.  */
214:				if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
215:					memset(data, 0, len);
216:				return MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED;
217:			}
218:
219:			vaddr += head;
220:			data += head;
221:			len -= head;
222:			offset = 0;
223:		}
224:	}
225:
226:	return MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE(cpu, mem, vaddr, data, len, writeflag, misc_flags);
227:}

231:	unsigned char *data, size_t len, int writeflag, int misc_flags)

451:		 *	b) offsets 0x124..0x777 are a device

454:		 *	   added to the dyntrans system as a "RAM" page

833: *  store_buf():
834: *
835: *  memcpy()-like helper function, from host RAM to emulated RAM.
836: */
837:void store_buf(struct cpu *cpu, uint64_t addr, const char *s, size_t len)
838:{
839:	size_t psize = 1024;	/*  1024 256 64 16 4 1  */
840:
841:	while (len != 0) {
842:		if ((addr & (psize-1)) == 0) {
843:			while (len >= psize) {
844:				cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, addr,
845:				    (unsigned char *)s, psize, MEM_WRITE,
846:				    CACHE_DATA);
847:				addr += psize;
848:				s += psize;
849:				len -= psize;
850:			}
851:		}
852:		psize >>= 2;
853:	}
854:
855:	while (len-- != 0)
856:		store_byte(cpu, addr++, *s++);
857:}

[35m[3mcodex[0m[0m
The table proves `{mc2, kbc1024}` is the minimum set for the listed escapes: only `mc2` detects `vt2`, and only `kbc1024` detects `vw256`. It does not prove coverage of the whole width/lane class.

F13 also needs a narrower statement. For a 16-byte request, an address aligned to 4 bytes but not 16 produces four 4-byte writes. Sixteen byte writes occur when the start is not 4-byte aligned.

[35m[3mcodex[0m[0m
The proposed `mc2` row has a narrower construction guarantee than the packet claims. A 32-byte request produces `16 + 16`; a 20-byte request produces `16 + 4`. Both forward source bytes `s[2]` and `s[19]` to the same ports, so those readbacks cannot prove the second chunk was wide. This is a static derivation from `store_buf`, not a new emulator measurement.

The contract explicitly discloses widths 64/256/1024, so I would not classify the width ladder as in-contract solely from “any.” But `vc`, `ve`, `vg`, `vt1`, and `vt2` escape at `C=16`, which has no such exclusion. Those breaches require an in-round repair; narrowing the header alone does not resolve them.

VERDICT: L2-CLASS-CLOSURE -- measured escapes at C=16 breach the shipped contract; narrowing that contract cannot discharge the resulting L2.

I revise my earlier position: the quoted pass-6 ruling does **not by itself establish an obligation to row all eight width/lane cells**. The decisive evidence is the committed contract and clause (e).

I also refine F5. The contract explicitly lists widths 64/256/1024 under “DISCLOSED, NOT CAUGHT.” I read that specific exclusion as limiting its broad “any guard” promise. Thus `va`, `vb`, and the width ladder are debt under that contract, with F4 correcting their erroneous justification. But `vc`, `ve`, `vg`, `vt1`, and `vt2` have measured escapes at **C=16**, for which there is no corresponding exclusion. Those establish L2 independently of the larger-width dispute. [Committed contract](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/lca_widewrite_probe.py:33)

|---|---|---|
| F1 | **UPHOLD** | Accept the supplied 102-leg measurements; I did not rerun them. |
| F2 | **UPHOLD** | Independently derived 8+2+1+1 = twelve cells; projecting away k gives eight, but does not establish equivalence against k-sensitive variants. |
| F3 | **UPHOLD** | Identify the measured `(16, k0, lane 3)` cell; the unqualified coverage statement overstates it. |
| F4 | **UPHOLD** | The threshold ladder directly defeats the shared-predicate justification. |
| F5 | **REFINE** | The C=16 breaches are L2 and require detection; larger-width membership is limited by clause (iii), and narrowing alone cannot repair the established breaches. |
| F6 | **UPHOLD** | A process-ending call during GETENV prevents the t4 print, making `wide getenv` red alongside `wide alive`. |
| F7 | **UPHOLD** | “Round-trip control” is accurate; the subsequent `0x44`, versus ordinary RAM’s first byte `0x41`, supplies the RAM discriminator. |
| F8 | **UPHOLD** | `0x7fe0 % 64 == 32`, while it is 16-aligned; a3=64 cannot make that start issue a 64-byte chunk. |
| F9 | **CANNOT SETTLE independently** | The packet supplies a census; the six permitted files cannot establish the absence of every other producer or bus master. |
| F10 | **REFINE** | The original a3=4 construction escape is real; `mc2` pins some wide activity, but its two stated readbacks do not pin both chunks to 16 bytes. |
| F11 | **REFINE** | The proposed order protects against the listed clobbering variants; separate-session measurements do not establish general soundness for arbitrary variants in a combined session. |
| F12 | **UPHOLD** | Normal completion after FAIL exits zero, and the oracle checks byte selection rather than delivery of the remaining bytes. |
| F13 | **REFINE** | Sixteen byte writes require a start that is **not 4-aligned**; a 4-aligned but non-16-aligned start produces four 4-byte writes. |

| D2 | **UPHOLD** | `wide alive` cannot be alone-red for the stated crash during GETENV. |
| D3 | **UPHOLD the refinement** | Scratch storage provides a round-trip control, not device-specific behavior by itself. |

The accepted disposition that is materially wrong is **F5’s remedy**: accepting an in-contract breach and then merely narrowing the contract contradicts the supplied clause-(e) rule. Its inclusion of `va`/`vb` also needs qualification because of the existing larger-width exclusion.

Under my mixed ruling, the bounded C=16 lane closure should use **`{l0w16, low16, mc2}`**, with the construction check strengthened:

- `l0w16`: lane 0.
- `low16`: lane 1 and the measured low-port refusal.
- `mc2`: lanes 2 and 3, k=16, and the continuation refusal.

These cover all four C=16 lanes and detect every listed C=16 contract breach. Separate sessions preserve the supplied measurement conditions.

`{mc2, kbc1024}` is indeed the minimum set detecting **all listed escapes**: only `mc2` detects `vt2`, and only `kbc1024` detects `vw256` in the table. But that is a minimum for the listed variants, not proof of eight-cell class closure. It exercises only three wide width/lane cells. Under my contract reading, the larger-width strengthening remains filed debt.

Two substantive omissions emerged from verification.

**First, `mc2` has a remaining construction ambiguity.** Applying the actual `store_buf` loop gives:

| Requested length | Callback lengths | Bytes forwarded to the two ports |
|---|---|---|
| 32 | 16, 16 | `s[2]`, `s[19]` |
| 20 | 16, 4 | `s[2]`, `s[19]` |

The two specified port readbacks therefore cannot distinguish these constructions. With 20 bytes, the described `vt2` no longer encounters a **wide** continuation. This does not invalidate the supplied 32-byte measurement; it limits the claim that the row pins its complete chunk sequence by construction. [Chunking implementation](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/core/memory.c:837)

The additional measurement I would name is **the weakened `mc2` request of 20 bytes against base and `vt2`**, checking callback lengths alongside the existing assertions. The unchanged forwarded bytes are statically established; the complete score depends on the unprovided `mc2` assertions.

**Second, `vh` has a stronger equivalence argument than the packet gives.** `memory_rw` zeroes the entire read buffer before dispatching the device. Since `ofs <= 3`, limiting the handler’s fill loop to eight bytes leaves an already-zero tail. Thus `vh` remains equivalent on reads through this dispatcher even if a wide-read producer exists. A direct-call harness lacking that initialization could distinguish it while failing to represent the normal calling context. [Read-buffer initialization](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:485)

I read only the six permitted files and ran no gates or detectors. The local files still contain the old contract, coverage sentence, and device-signature label, so I treat their correction as owed rather than verified complete.

`{mc2, kbc1024}` is indeed the minimum set detecting **all listed escapes**: only `mc2` detects `vt2`, and only `kbc1024` detects `vw256` in the table. But that is a minimum for the listed variants, not proof of eight-cell class closure. It exercises only three wide width/lane cells. Under my contract reading, the larger-width strengthening remains filed debt.

Two substantive omissions emerged from verification.

**First, `mc2` has a remaining construction ambiguity.** Applying the actual `store_buf` loop gives:

| Requested length | Callback lengths | Bytes forwarded to the two ports |
|---|---|---|
| 32 | 16, 16 | `s[2]`, `s[19]` |
| 20 | 16, 4 | `s[2]`, `s[19]` |

The two specified port readbacks therefore cannot distinguish these constructions. With 20 bytes, the described `vt2` no longer encounters a **wide** continuation. This does not invalidate the supplied 32-byte measurement; it limits the claim that the row pins its complete chunk sequence by construction. [Chunking implementation](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/core/memory.c:837)

The additional measurement I would name is **the weakened `mc2` request of 20 bytes against base and `vt2`**, checking callback lengths alongside the existing assertions. The unchanged forwarded bytes are statically established; the complete score depends on the unprovided `mc2` assertions.

**Second, `vh` has a stronger equivalence argument than the packet gives.** `memory_rw` zeroes the entire read buffer before dispatching the device. Since `ofs <= 3`, limiting the handler’s fill loop to eight bytes leaves an already-zero tail. Thus `vh` remains equivalent on reads through this dispatcher even if a wide-read producer exists. A direct-call harness lacking that initialization could distinguish it while failing to represent the normal calling context. [Read-buffer initialization](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:485)

I read only the six permitted files and ran no gates or detectors. The local files still contain the old contract, coverage sentence, and device-signature label, so I treat their correction as owed rather than verified complete.
